Built: the game runs on three 0.187.0 with the WebGL renderer and draws what it drew on 0.185.1. Three's changed lighting, shadow lookups, environment filter and bloom blur are held to 0.185's (twelve holds, 0.185's `PMREMGenerator` carried, the bloom's blur material; the lesson above), three workarounds 0.187 made needless are gone, and `threeAnchors.test.ts` names every place the game matches three's text or reaches under it, so the next upgrade says what moved. Its review found one break every gate had passed: the water's mask twins still called three's `computeMultiscattering` as 0.185 declared it and compiled in no browser on 0.187, so with Effects on every lake and sea had lost its reflection; the call is mended, and the test now holds every function of three's that the game's shader text calls and puts each injected program together whole, the water's own among them (the lesson above). Measured only in headless Chrome of our own, the two builds side by side in one session (0.185 from main at 87280ee), never on the owner's machine. The picture, read off the effects' scene target on Direct3D 11 at fourteen kinds of view (a Mos Eisley street at noon, rooms, Theed and its lake, a Yavin 4 forest, Tatooine's far rings, Mustafar's lava, Tatooine's orbit, night with a lit saber, forced rain, the volumetrics master on, a world of imported models, the select screen, the ship edit page, the map) and at four of them again on Vulkan: every view differs between the builds by no more than two sessions of 0.185 differ, or by under one pixel in a hundred (the street 4.0% of pixels by more than two levels of 255 against 2.8% between two sessions of 0.185, all of it the cloud sheets, the figure, the lamps and the animated screens; a room 0.26% and 0.08%; the forest 0.44%; the orbit 0.73%). Water, whose reflection is the effects' own pass and so in no scene target, was held after the mend on the whole frame from above its surface, the pass drawing, in sessions started together: Theed's lake from its bank at noon, at a grazing angle, across it, under a low sun and at night differs between the builds over 0.16 to 0.87% of the frame and by more than two levels over 0.03 to 0.08% (mean under a hundredth of a level), with the volumetrics master on (the current variant and its twins) over 0.15% below the sky, and Corellia's swelling sea from its shore over 7 pixels of 1.3 million at noon and 0.68% under a low sun against 0.48% between two sessions of 0.185; on Vulkan three of the lake's views differ over 0.94 to 1.62% (0.10 to 0.38% by more than two levels, at shadow and geometry edges, none of it on the water). Every console self-test answers alike on both builds, every value within 0.2%, with reversed depth on and, for the grass's and the leaves' shadow tests, off; `fxCheck` is clean and no shader error stands in either build's console at every water view (before the mend 0.187's was not clean wherever water was drawn). The cost, in turns: the frame's median at Bestine, on a Mos Eisley street and in a room 19.6, 17.1 and 13.3 ms on 0.185 against 19.6, 17.0 and 13.1 on 0.187 on Direct3D 11, and 17.3, 18.2 and 14.5 against 17.6, 18.9 and 14.2 on Vulkan (each the mean of the builds in either of two tabs); the 0.2 ms more first read in the street's world pass on 0.187 did not hold up: measured again in review with the people out and each view waited on until its draw count stood still, the builds stand within about a millisecond of each other either way round at a town, a room and a forest on both backends (the world pass 6.51 against 6.45 ms on the street on Direct3D 11 and 6.59 against 6.79 on Vulkan), which is what two tabs differ by, and no steady cost of 0.187's was found; after the mend, over water, the frame's median is 6.5 against 6.6 ms at Theed's lake and 8.3 against 8.3 at Corellia's sea, the effects' pass on the graphics card 0.87 against 1.07 ms at the lake and 0.49 against 0.50 at the sea; an arrival's screen 20.7 against 20.1 s on Direct3D 11 with 114 programs each, and four arrivals 59.1 against 63.9 s on Vulkan and 58.9 against 56.8 s with the builds swapped between the tabs; a two-minute run round Bestine built two programs in play on both builds on both backends, with no frame over the link-wait line on Direct3D 11 and two on Vulkan on both; the light churn nought; the textures, geometries and the effects' 60 MB alike; and a sky capture still makes no new target (one made after more than a hundred and fifty captures). Not held, and so three's new look the day something uses it: sheen, iridescence, anisotropy, transmission, light probes, area lights, diffuse roughness, and a Lambert or Phong material under an environment. Held though it was 0.185's own fault: the sun's shadow bias added under reversed depth. Nothing of it has been seen by eye: whether any surface, shadow edge, reflection or glow looks other than it did is the owner's to judge.

**WebGPU behind a switch** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's third step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP3.md`, both makers' halves: the switch, WebGL kept, the game on WebGPU, the stand-ins, the portal passes, the sun's cascades, the console and the previews; then the warm-up, the region tiers' render bundles and the four private patches of three's; then a fix round). Built: `?renderer=webgpu` (kept on the address) or `localStorage['swg.renderer'] = 'webgpu'` and a reload draws the game with three's `WebGPURenderer` on its WebGPU backend, and where WebGPU cannot start the game draws on WebGL and says so once on the message line. WebGL was the default until 2026-11-18 (WebGPU is now, `?renderer=webgl` the old one) and is unchanged: its boot loads nothing of the port but the switch (751 of the game's modules, none of them WebGPU's), and on a Mos Eisley street, its cantina and Theed's lake every program it built is one the build before built, source for source with the material's name taken out, but for a passing variant of a material both builds draw (the fix round's two passes over the route: 397 of 398 alike each way, a variant of an antenna's program in this build alone and of a field of points' in the one before, and the variants of both held at the end alike), with no shader error and `fxCheck` clean. On WebGPU every classic material draws through three's own node copy of it, with none of our injections (the client's highlight, the wet look, the detail maps, the leaf cards' light, the sway); the sky dome, the lava and the ground's tile program are plain stand-ins (a gradient from the hour's horizon colour to its zenith, the ramp's middle colour, the colour map's tint over a grey; dials in `GPU_LOOK`, `__debug.gpu({ look })`); the doorways' depth reset is its own stand-in, so a room and the street through its door draw as on WebGL, a room seen through its door from the street included (by day such a room is unlit on both renderers, the rooms' pool lighting only the building the camera is in, and WebGL's sky reflection on a metal cabinet is all that shows there, which WebGPU, capturing no reflection this step, leaves black; by the torch at night it is the same room on both, `s3-fix-door-torch-gpu.png` and `-gl.png`); the sun's three cascades are three's `CSMShadowNode` at the game's own breaks, distance, bias and softness, and a room's lamps cast when the setting asks; the water is drawn flat (its simulation and its reflection held off); the plain dome of a world with no converted sky stands in as one colour, the fog's; and every other `ShaderMaterial` draws nothing and is named by `__debug.gpu()`, by its own name or its first uniforms where it has none: every particle effect, every lit lightsaber blade, the cloud sheets, the stars, the grass, the marks, the room motes, and in space the nebulae. Held off and said so: the effects chain, the far rings, the volumetrics, the shadow hold and batches, the leaf skip. The previews (the doll, the select screen, the ship edit page, the map's pictures) still draw on WebGL in contexts of their own. Every pipeline a loading screen's sweep, a body made ready or a tier streaming in would need is listed by three's own walk, every sun cascade's included, and built off the frame (`gpu({ warm })`); the region tiers' still instanced meshes draw from one render bundle a tier (about 1,250 draws to 510 on the Mos Eisley street, `gpu({ bundles })`), recorded again when a mesh's copies, its material or its pictures change; a still caster's shadow program is not refreshed in every cascade every frame (`gpu({ patches: { shadow } })`); and the game's dynamic-draw hint is dropped, so what is rewritten in play is written to the GPU when it changes (`gpu({ dynamicHint })`). The fix round found that nothing rewritten in play had been reaching the GPU after its first frame (a level group's copies, the flora batches, a folded row hidden in place: the lesson above) and mended it. Measured in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080, the two renderers in turns in one session (the idle tab frozen, three turns, absolute times wandering by half again between runs on that machine, so only turns compare): on the Mos Eisley street WebGL's frame median 7.9 to 9.7 ms and its render calls 4.7 to 5.3 ms against WebGPU's 11.1 to 12.7 and 10.1 to 10.4 (draws 660 to 820 against 510 to 530), WebGPU with its bundles off 15.8 to 18.4 ms of render calls, its shadow patch off 9.9 to 12.3, its still and warm patches off within the noise (8.3 to 10.9; the still patch tells nothing this step and the warm one changes only compiles), its stencil patch off (the bundles held off with it) 15.1 to 16.7, and the dynamic hint kept 16.7 to 20.8; on Bestine's square WebGL's render calls 6.0 to 7.0 ms against WebGPU's 6.3 to 10.2, bundles off 10.8 to 13.6, hint kept 13.4 to 14.8. After an arrival on a profile that had been there before, a two-minute walk of teleports along the Mos Eisley street made no pipeline at a draw on WebGPU (677 made ahead of their draws, 8.3 s of the main thread's node building spread over it; frame median 9.0 ms, p99 37, 33 frames over 50 ms, worst 241) where WebGL built 22 programs in play (median 9.0, p99 26, 22 frames over 50 ms, worst 135); a first arrival on a cold profile waits minutes behind its screen (pipelines land about one a second); still made at a draw in play, now and then (none to thirteen over a session of every view below): the flora's batches and a held weapon the warm-up is never handed, a piece of a building's furniture drawn in a room's pass it was warmed only for the world's, and on reaching Tatooine's orbit by `travel` dozens more (66 once, the sky's bodies warmed only for the rooms' pass). The patch telling a still thing's program it carries no node tells nothing yet (every classic material's node copy carries none). Measured only in headless Chrome of our own on an RTX 5080 (Chrome's own WebGPU, Direct3D 12): the select screen, a Mos Eisley street (about 400 to 1,000 draws and 0.35 to 1 million triangles a frame), the cantina from inside, a player house's room seen through its door from the street, Bestine, a landspeeder ridden 265 m, an X-wing taken to Tatooine's orbit by `travel`, flown there at 650 km/h and brought down by the ship menu's land, Theed's lake, a Yavin 4 forest, Mustafar's lava, a death and coming round, the ship edit page, the map's two pictures and the Graphics page, with no uncaught error and no WebGPU error; screenshots `A:\swg3js\port\shots\s3-*.png`. The console's warnings on WebGPU alone: three's "Vertex attribute uv not found" twice on reaching a space zone (the space dust's points: WebGPU draws a point a pixel and finds no coordinate to sample its picture by, and the stars a pixel too), Chrome's "Draw with an index count of 0" once (a far tile whose cut keeps no quad: three's WebGPU submits the empty draw WebGL skips), and Chrome's note that it ignores `powerPreference` on Windows; the X4122 warnings of the ship edit page's WebGL preview and three's PropertyBinding warnings in the cantina come on WebGL alike. The picture is plainer than WebGL's (no grade, no effects, no client highlight, no sky reflections) and Mustafar much redder. Not built: the effects, the far rings, the volumetrics, the water's look, the grass, the marks and every injected look on WebGPU (later steps, and the remakes that are the owner's). Nothing of it has been seen by the owner; whether WebGPU becomes the game's renderer is his to decide in his visible Chrome.

**The models' own looks on WebGPU** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's fourth step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP4.md`, its first maker's half: the client's highlight, the reflections and the detail maps). Built: on WebGPU every converted model draws the client's own highlight (each shader's mode, colour, power and mask, the band, the cap, the gate and `spec({ use: 'three' })` live), our sky's reflection on every registered material (captured every four seconds into one kept cube by three's WebGPU generator, the black stand-in while a world has none, the 'game' source through a generator of its own) and the detail maps on the second coordinate set; each is on, `__debug.gpu({ look: { spec, env, detail } })` takes it off live and `localStorage['swg.gpu.spec']` (or `env`, `detail`) `= '0'` with a reload leaves it out at load. WebGL is unchanged: over a Mos Eisley street, its cantina and Theed's lake every program the build before built is one this build built. Step 3's stand-ins read their colours and dials in the shared render group now, which mends every WebGPU reflection being the first capture's sky. `__debug.specSelfTest()` on WebGPU draws the client's term within 0.003 of the mirror, and the switch to three's GGX and the look switch exactly, building no pipeline. Measured only in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080, picture pairs against WebGL at the same views with each look on and off (a house wall, a block's wall with a detail map, a hull with a reflection cube at Mos Eisley and at Theed): over the pixels each look moves, the look on stands nearer WebGL's picture on 69 to 89% of them for the detail maps, 59 to 76% for the reflection and 32 to 65% for the highlight (three 0.187's diffuse and its own filter of the environment are not 0.185's, and the sky captured is step 3's stand-in); the doll drew with no error. Not built: the wet film's weather cases (the wetness is a remake of the owner's), the 'scene' comparison of the dome, the water's reflection, the previews' own studio (still WebGL), and the rest of the step (the second maker's). Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**The leaf cards on WebGPU, what the node copies carry, and nothing built in play** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's fourth step, its second maker's half). Built: on WebGPU every leaf card draws the client's own light (the ramp about the sun's axis, the ambient alone for its indirect light, the emissive and the fills, carried back through the inverse of three's tone curve before three's own tone mapping, which on WebGPU comes after it in three's output pass), the sun's shadow taken from the cascades' own lookup and fade to lower its front row, the stored mirrored backs, `pointFlat` under a point light, the rooms' pass putting the card light out for its own draws, a blended card's alpha raised by its mip level and its cut where three makes it, and the leaf skip (the lookup moved toward the sun by the ray's way out of its own crown, and the acne floor on every cascade's bias); `__debug.gpu({ look: { card: false } })` takes it off live and `localStorage['swg.gpu.card'] = '0'` with a reload leaves it out at load, which also holds the leaf skip off. `__debug.cardLightSelfTest()` and `leafShadowSelfTest()` run on WebGPU through the node graph against `cardLightMath.ts` and `leafShadow.ts` (within a tenth of a byte, the skip's three cases as WebGL's). `__debug.gpu()` says what stays as step 3 left it (`stays`) and `gpu({ carries: true })` checks what three's node copies carry of the scene's materials (the person keys, the emissive splits, unlit, decals, flip-books and scrolls, the stencil reference: all carried wherever met, at Mos Eisley, Theed and a Naboo forest). The flora stand-ins are built as the batches draw, receiving the sun's shadow, so a species first met in play no longer builds its pipeline at a draw. Measured only in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080 (Direct3D 12): WebGL's programs over a Mos Eisley street, its cantina and Theed's lake are 1446f2a's (every one of its programs built, one more each run that a plain material compiled before its cascades' adoption accounts for, and that run's base built the first run's extra itself), with no shader error and `fxCheck()` clean; at a Naboo forest view the card part on stands nearer WebGL's picture on 86% of the pixels it moves; two-minute walks after a warm arrival made one pipeline at a draw at Mos Eisley (a pistol a person drew), none at Theed and three in a Naboo forest (a ShaderMaterial's stand-in and a blaster bolt's two materials), none of them a card's; switching each look off moved neither the frame nor the render call's time beyond the noise. Not built: a held weapon drawn in play, a bolt's colour and a ShaderMaterial's stand-in still build at their first draw (step 3's warm-up never sees them); the flora's sway and wetness are still the owner's remakes, so no card sways on WebGPU; and a long session that had travelled once drew no near flora batch at a Naboo view, which a fresh session at the same view did and which is not understood. Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**The fourth step's fix round** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main). The review's brighter sunlit cards on WebGPU are WebGL's fault, not the port's: with reversed depth WebGL's card shadow lookup is turned round (its offset, acne floor and skip all push away from the sun, and its own `leafShadowSelfTest()` fails there), while WebGPU's matches WebGL's with `?norevz` to within 0.7 of a level on average over a Naboo forest's cards; it is not mended here, since the mend is a WebGL line. Not mended: people's clothes built at a draw in a building's rooms pass while a town settles after a teleport (eleven pieces at Mos Eisley, all clones of looks the warm-up never listed, drawn in the rooms' proxy scene: the review's stormtrooper case, now named by its pass); a weapon a person draws, a bolt's or a basic material's first draw, which step 3's warm-up never sees. Measured only in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080 (Direct3D 12 and ANGLE Direct3D 11). Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**The ground and the far rings on WebGPU** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's fifth step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP5.md`, its first maker's half: items 1 and 2). WebGL is 28fdb7c's: over Mos Eisley's street, its cantina, Theed and Mustafar's lava 404 of 28fdb7c's 405 program sources are this build's (the eighteen others named for the props and people each run stood, the ground's and the ring's among the 404), with no shader error and the effects clean. Measured only in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080 on Direct3D 12 against WebGL on ANGLE Direct3D 11: Mos Eisley's street differs from WebGL by 0.45 of a level on average, every one of `__debug.ground`'s switches moves both renderers alike (the shine 0.88 against 1.08, the mirror 12.3 against 11.8, the client's tint 62.3 against 62.1, the channels 8.3 against 8.1, the opposite rule, the masks, the order, the side pictures and the relief within a few tenths), and the rings' own pixels behind Mos Eisley differ by 2.9 of a level on average. Mended in the step's fix round: from 500 m over Naboo the rings on WebGPU drew a thin dashed black line along every ridge past the fine extent where WebGL drew none, a NaN normal from the flat face taken inside a branch (the lesson above); it is gone, and the ridges there differ from WebGL by 2.3 levels on average. **The lava, the marks, the blades, the particles and the tunnel on WebGPU** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's fifth step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP5.md`, its second maker's half: items 3 to 7). Built: on WebGPU the client's lava (the noise volume, the ramp, the crust and the glow, three's own fog), the marks (both sheets), the lightsaber ribbons, the client's particles with their ribbons, flip-books and solid quads, the weather's falling effects in their own variant, and the hyperspace tunnel are each drawn by a node graph of its own (`src/core/gpu/gpuPrograms.ts`) that reads the drawn material's own uniforms at every draw, each on unless `localStorage['swg.gpu.lava']` (or `marks`, `blades`, `particles`, `tunnel`) `= '0'` with a reload; `__debug.gpu()` says each graph's count (`programs`) and what the port leaves for the owner's remakes and step 6 (`notPorted`: the sky, water, clouds, volumetrics, wetness, sway, grass and the far tree cards, Force lightning and every Force power's own effects, the vehicles' dust, the rooms' motes and beams, the previews' depth of field and every effect pass). No other `ShaderMaterial` the game draws is outside that list. On WebGPU alone: a modulate particle batch blends by the client's own rule (three refuses the multiply it was given on both renderers; WebGL's is left as it stands), a batch of the world's carries the portal renderer's stencil from its making, the weather has twins of its warm batches in both of its stencils, and the noise volume goes up with no mip chain (three's WebGPU cannot build one for a 3D texture). WebGL is 28fdb7c's: over Mos Eisley's street, its cantina, Theed and Mustafar's lava 415 of 28fdb7c's 416 program sources are this build's (the hyperspace tunnel's and the lava's among them; the base's other a second variant of the heat haze's lava program differing only by `HAS_NORMAL`, for a lava mesh that run met; this build's two others a point cloud and a prop its run stood), with no shader error and the effects clean. Measured only in headless tabs of our own on the RTX 5080 on Direct3D 12 against WebGL on ANGLE Direct3D 11, the effects off: two-minute walks after a warm arrival -- Mos Eisley's street, a Naboo forest, Theed, Mustafar's lava, a lit blade swung, the DL-44 fired at fighters, and hyperspace jumps across Tatooine's orbit -- made no pipeline at a draw for any of the step's own programs (0 on the street, Theed, the lava, the blade and the jumps; the forest's 2 and the fight's 3 were basic materials, a room's clothes and an invisible collider, step 3's and step 4's known gaps); the marks differ from WebGL by 2.0 levels on average; the lava draws its noise, ramp and crust (32.6 on average against a WebGL frame of another moment of the flow), and its glow and brightness switches move both renderers alike (11.7 against 11.0, 15.8 against 19.9); the tunnel's glow switch moves both alike (30.5 against 30.4). Each item's cost, read in turns off WebGPU's own timestamp queries (`renderer.backend.trackTimestamp`, the median render-pass time of 40 frames hidden and shown, six rounds, 1584 by 805), where the frame's pacing showed nothing: the near ground 0.08 ms and the rings 0.15 ms on Mos Eisley's street, a brazier's fire and smoke 0.08 ms, the marks, a lit blade, the lava and the tunnel each within 0.01 ms of nought (the lava costs no more than the ground it covers, the tunnel no more than the world it hides). The fix round took the lava's crust and ramp samples out of the crust's branch (the lesson above). Not mended: an additive fire blends in linear light on WebGPU, where WebGL tone-maps each quad and adds the shown values, so a brazier's fire draws much fainter on WebGPU than on WebGL, which no graph can carry; a blue blade's glow drifts violet through the inverse of three's tone curve; and two `THREE.AttributeNode: Vertex attribute "uv" not found` warnings, met in Mos Eisley and in space, come of no graph of this step's (every geometry they draw carries its `uv`) and were not traced. **The effects chain's bones, the bloom and the colour grade on WebGPU** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's sixth step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP6.md`, its first maker's half: items 1 to 3). Built: on WebGPU with Effects on the scene is drawn into the chain's own linear half-float target, the sanitize zeroes what is not a number and holds the brightest at 256, the bloom (three's pass with 0.185's blur, five levels) and the colour grade (the planet's look, the hour, the rooms' share) are the WebGL passes' own objects each drawn by node graphs over their own uniforms, and three's output pass tone-maps at the game's exposure and encodes sRGB; the Effects setting, the bloom's strength and the grade's knobs move live, and Effects switched in play goes behind the WebGL path's own notice and builds nothing at its first frame; with Effects off the scene goes to the canvas as step 5 left it. `localStorage['swg.gpu.chain']` (or `bloom`, `grade`) `= '0'` with a reload leaves the chain, or one pass, out at load, and `__debug.gpu().chain` says what is drawn, what each graph binds and the switches. **The ambient occlusion and the fire on WebGPU** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's sixth step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP6.md`, its second maker's half: items 4 and 5, the words for what is not ported, and the step's proof). Built: on WebGPU with Effects on the ambient occlusion is the WebGL pass's own object drawn by node graphs over its own uniforms, half or full resolution, with its sky and rooms' light sets read from the portal stencil, the torch, the rooms' pool (its spots and casting points), the sun's cascades taken off WebGPU's own sun, its two products (the half distance and the half normals) and the debug view, so `__debug.ssao({ split, view })` works as on WebGL; it is kept off the water by drawing the water's twins' geometry into its region target, which WebGL reads from the water mask product WebGPU has not got (`__debug.ssao().water.webgpu`). With Effects off every material is now tone-mapped and encoded in its own output and summed on the canvas, as WebGL draws it (the inline output), so a brazier's flames, sparks and glows and its smoke add up as on WebGL, and the blue blade's glow no longer drifts violet. `localStorage['swg.gpu.ssao']` or `['swg.gpu.inline']` `= '0'` with a reload leaves either out; `__debug.gpu({ inline: false })` moves the inline output live. `gpu()` and every Graphics row of a pass not ported say "not on WebGPU yet", naming the blade glow and its shadows among them. Measured only in headless tabs of our own (WebGPU on Direct3D 12, WebGL on ANGLE Direct3D 11): the occlusion's own views against WebGL's differ by a mean of 0.05 to 0.73 levels on Mos Eisley's street, the cantina and Theed's lake (the multiplier white over the water on both), and 0.39 to 0.47 on Mustafar but for a band of far ground at the horizon that WebGPU's far cascade takes for shadowed (its fraction 0.85 against 0.14, the sun's own cascades, not this step's); a brazier's flames at night, their brightest twentieth 255 on WebGL with Effects off and 255 on WebGPU with the inline output, 206 without; the blue blade at Effects off red over blue 0.128 on WebGL, 0.13 with the inline output, 0.305 without; the occlusion 0.08 ms in turns off WebGPU's timestamps at 1600 by 900 against WebGL's 0.126 (with its products 0.147); the Effects switch in play, after the step's fixer, built no pipeline at a draw in four switches of a fresh session, in four switched straight after a teleport into Mos Eisley (51 before the fix, where WebGL built two programs in play), and in six after the inline output's console toggles (whose own first draws build the canvas's output, as said), the chain's own graphs none at any (the water's coverage was built at every new chain's first frame with water in view before); two-minute walks at Mos Eisley, Theed, Mustafar's mining cantina and its lava built no pipeline at a draw from this step's graphs; and over the street, the cantina, Theed and the lava all 424 of 8d0c759's WebGL program sources are this build's, `fxCheck` clean. Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**The WebGPU frame's main thread** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's performance step, `A:\swg3js\port\STEP-PERF.md`, its first maker's half: items 1 and 2). Built: the frame report on WebGPU, `await __debug.gpu({ perf: true })` (`src/core/gpu/framePerf.ts`): the main thread and its sections with the sun's three cascades timed on their own, a second window counting a frame's render calls and passes, objects drawn, refreshes by kind, pipeline and bind-group switches, buffers set, bundles replayed and recorded, bytes written, submits and anything made, then the card's time where three's timestamps answer; and two switches that take WebGPU's main thread down, each on, its off step 6's draw (`src/core/gpu/drawCost.ts`): `replay` (`patchReplay.ts`, a fifth private patch of three's, pinned in `patchTable.ts`) asks three about a bundled object only where its answer could be other than nothing, and `shadowCull` (`shadowCull.ts`) culls what casts no shadow from a shadow pass before three tests, lists and sorts it. Measured only in a headless tab of ours on the RTX 5080 at 1600x900 with the frames held to 72 a second, both on against both off, interleaved in one tab: the draw's main-thread time 0.37 ms less at Mos Eisley's street, 0.22 at Bestine, 0.18 at Kachirho, 0.12 in the cantina, 0.11 at Theed and nothing at Mustafar; profiled at the street, three's `needsRefresh` 0.22 ms a frame less and the cascades' listing 0.1 to 0.14 less. Before the step, so held, WebGPU's frame stood at or under WebGL's at most views and over it at Bestine (p50 4.8 against 4.4 ms) and Mustafar (4.0 against 3.4); the machine was too busy afterwards (the owner's own Chrome, another worktree's tests) for a fair absolute after at every view, so the switches' worth is the interleaved figures. Telling the plain shadow programs they carry no node measured nothing and was taken back out. Picture pairs at the street: the switches alternating differ by no more than the scene's own motion between two shots. Not built: the cascades' cadence, fewer render calls, the render-group buffers written once a frame and the bones once a frame, which are the gap left and the next maker's to weigh. Nothing of it has been seen by the owner. Its second maker (items 3 to 5): a third switch, `sweepPace` (on, its off step 6's pace), paces the Effects switch's sweep in play on WebGPU by a slice of listing a frame where step 6 waited a frame after every batch of eight: at Mos Eisley's street the switch took 2.4 to 3.5 s on a quiet machine (11.3 s the session's first) where it took 13.5 s, and WebGL's 13.4 to 13.6 s (41 s its first), with nothing built at a draw. Arrivals, cold and warm profiles, in headless tabs of ours: Tatooine's first arrival 23 to 28 s on WebGPU against 52 to 73 s on WebGL cold and 26 to 30 s against 32 s warm, a cold travel to Naboo about 33 s against 50 s, with no pipeline built at a draw; two of three WebGPU arrivals on Naboo straight after a reload waited out the warm-up's 30 s landing bound (82 and 93 s against 29 s and WebGL's 71.5 s), which is not mended. Four two-minute walks (the street, the cantina, Theed, Mustafar's lava) built no pipeline at a draw. The climbing course stood on WebGPU with nothing built, a body walked the stairs and climbed a wall as on WebGL, and the climbing's own cost a frame (the `player` section) is the same code on both, 0.2 to 0.6 ms; grabbed with E (the use key, the grab's own), a braced lip hangs and a wall climbs alike on both renderers (the fixer's run; this maker's runs landed no grab, and Space, a Jedi's Force jump, grabs nothing). The frame on a quiet machine: WebGPU at or under WebGL's median at the street and the cantina, 0.4 ms over at Bestine, and 3 ms over on the street looking low across the climbing course (not isolated); under load at Mustafar and Kachirho well under WebGL's, at Theed and the forest 0.5 to 1 ms over at the median with a better p95; `A:\swg3js\port\PERF-findings.md` is the written finding for the remakes. Its fixer: the card's time on WebGPU was three's own answer, often a frame's first few passes; it is now every timed pass of whole frames (`completeCardFrames`), 0.8 to 1.8 ms at the step's views, and the report says which passes go untimed (three's `clear()` as the frame begins); WebGL's timer queries count the main thread's issuing and are said beside it, never compared. In the fixer's quiet session (both renderers in one tab, the step's switches on): WebGPU's median at or under WebGL's at the street (6.0 against 6.2 ms), the cantina (5.0 against 5.2), the street over the climbing course, Theed (5.4 against a settled 5.8), the forest (4.7 against 4.6, even), Kachirho and space, 0.2 ms over at Mustafar's lava and 0.3 over at Bestine, where the cascades (1.30 against WebGL's shadow pass's 0.76) and the effects chain (0.38 against 0.20) outweigh a world pass 0.5 ms cheaper; the scene's walk, which the review read as a WebGPU premium, is the same cost on both in a settled tab and rises with a busy one. `shadowCull` alone is about 0.07 ms a frame at the street by the profiler and nothing measurable at Bestine: kept, and the owner's to keep or take out. Not mended: a same-world teleport can build a person's pipelines for a room's pass at a draw (the warm-up's, either way the switches stand), and three arrivals straight after a reload waited the warm-up's landing bound out (76 and 129 s on Tatooine, over 300 s on Mustafar). Nothing of it has been seen by the owner.

**Pass 1's first wave: the measures** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the GPU port's pass 1, wave 1, `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W1.md`, its first maker's half: items 1 to 7; nothing of the picture changes). Built: a ledger of every render pipeline three makes on WebGPU (`src/core/gpu/pipeLedger.ts`), each recorded as it is asked for and as it lands, with its material, its first object, its pass, who asked (the warm-up, a loading screen, the world's queue, a draw, a self-test) and the parts of three's key read off the render object; on it the key census, `__debug.gpu({ keys: true })` (`keyCensus.ts`: every pipeline, the field of three's key separating it from its nearest sibling, the count by field, and what the bone pool and the morph bake would merge), and the landing log, `__debug.gpu({ landing: true })` (each pipeline asked and landed since the page's start, by arrival, and what was pending 30 s into each, by name); the frame report's card time named by section (each sun cascade, the world pass, the rooms, the doorways, the chain's passes), every read of three's timestamps behind one adapter (`gpuTimer.ts`) so three r188's `GPUTimer` changes only it; and a node test holding the anchor-relative rule over every GPU file from pass 1 on (`tools/swg/tests/anchorScan.test.ts`). Measured: Tatooine's arrival at Mos Eisley holds 651 pipelines 30 s in, and a morph attribute alone separates 380 of them (every crowd body has a geometry of its own and three keys a program on its morph attributes), bone count 18; baking the morphs would take 348 away, the bone pool alone 15, both 370, so wave 4's target is at most 300; the bake costs about 1 MB at the street, Bestine and the cantina against 13 to 16 MB of morph textures held today; and no clip the game plays keys a morph weight (20,761 GLB files and the imported pack read: no `weights` channel). The numbers, the baselines at the four views on both renderers and the commands that made them are kept on the owner's machine (`A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-census.md`, `PASS1-baselines.md`). The wave's review found the anchor scan stopping at a call's first bracket and a statement's end, so a uniform made of a world matrix inside a nested call, the camera's place, an eye's components and a place reaching a 32-bit array a statement later all passed; it now reads each call's whole argument and follows a name filled from a world place to the end of its block, with every form the review found planted in its own check. Not seen by eye: nothing here draws.

**Pass 1's first wave: the small things** (on `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the same wave's second maker's half: items 8 to 11, which change both renderers alike). Built: **H hides the interface** (`hideHud`, rebindable on the Controls page, where the help's row was): `#ui` wears `ui-hidden` (not `hidden`, whose page rule would take the menus down too) and the stylesheet hides every child of it by `visibility` but a named list of what stays -- the panels and the map, the loading screen, the travel curtain, the death card, a conversation, the emote wheel, the debug menu, a trade's window, the creator's bar, the chat line, a group's question and the editor's window -- while the frame writes none of what it hides (each piece's `rest` closing its second of writes, so `__debug.hud()` reads the hidden second's own 0) and every piece is written afresh when it comes back (`refresh`: value records let go, two-state records taken from the page). Not kept across a reload; hidden stays hidden through travel; the world editor's H is still its key list, and the editor coming on shows the interface. `__debug.hud({ hidden })` does the same from the console. **The help panel is gone**, with its action (a saved `help` choice is dropped on load), its corner word and the kits' help lines. **The class words are gone** from the display, the creator (a new character is made with the Jedi kit) and the select screen (the list's line, the card's row and line), and the Interface page names its crosshair switch by the saber and the Force; C still switches the kit and a saved Bounty Hunter keeps it. **The address** keeps every switch as typed and takes an old link's `planet`, `zone` and `class` off (`src/core/address.ts`); the old one wrote over the whole address at each arrival and lost `?server=` and `?word=` on a reload. Measured in headless Chrome on the RTX 5080 (WebGPU through Direct3D 12; WebGL on ANGLE's Direct3D 11): H twice on foot, on a speeder bike and at an X-wing's controls on both renderers, in space on WebGPU, and hidden through a jump to orbit and back, each hidden second 0 writes by every counter and 0 overlay operations; WebGL's program sources at the street, the cantina, Theed and Mustafar's lava all among a `64a6438` build's (616 of 616, the base 3 more streamed in); on WebGPU a cold arrival at Mos Eisley and a two-minute walk built no pipeline by a draw. Not seen by eye: the hide key aboard a ship's rooms (not reached in the headless tab), and the owner's own look at all of it. Found and left: going into the creator and back and then playing leaves the character stuck at the creator's stage spot, on `64a6438` too. The review found the hide key's test holding only three of the records read back from the page on the way back; it now turns each one's truth while hidden, both ways, and each of the twenty-eight such records turned into a flip fails it.

**Pass 1's second wave: three at its development commit** (held on `claude/pass1-w2` until a browser has seen it, since every headless Chrome start of the wave was refused, then `claude/three-187`, not yet on main and never to reach it from the development branch; the GPU port's pass 1, wave 2, `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-W2.md`, its first maker's half: item 1). Not seen yet: everything in a browser (the start of a Chrome was refused this maker, so the bundles and the shadow warming in play, the census against 651, the frame report at the four views, the arrivals, the walks, the WebGL program comparison and every picture pair are owed), and the captured WebGL keys in `shaderWatch.test.ts` and `programCensus.test.ts` are the 0.187 captures (which the commit's build still makes, its key functions being 0.187's build's text) until retaken from the running game.

**Pass 1's second wave: where load time goes** (held on `claude/pass1-w2` until a browser has seen it, since every headless Chrome start of the wave was refused, then `claude/three-187`, not yet on main; the same wave's second maker's half: item 2, on both renderers, changing nothing drawn). Built: `__debug.load()` (`src/core/loadTimes.ts`), the last twelve journeys split by stage -- the page's start to the character list (its phases the boot's own steps) and every arrival or trip between worlds, a jump to space and a landing each one, begun and ended by the travel's flag at the moment any trip sets it (a setter on `App`). Each journey says its downloads by kind (packs, models, textures, terrain, sounds, the code: files, bytes over the wire and of the files, what came from the cache, and the wall time one was under way, from the browser's own timings folded into a kept ring each time its record fills; `index.html` gives the record room for the dev server's module graph), its stages from marks at their sites (each model from the loader's first hook to its last, the ground's pictures decoded and laid, a chunk's ground and a far tile, a chunk's plants, props and grass, every collider a physics world makes, a tier of placed objects and its rooms put together: each one's sum and union), its shaders (WebGPU: the landing log's pipelines, now also given unrounded by `landing({ exact })`, and the time one was pending; WebGL: the link waits and the screens' sweep), each of its loading screen's phases with all of those inside it, what is left, and the share that is shaders and the share that is not, by the screens' words and by measure. A mark between journeys reads one flag and allocates nothing (twelve processes). Not seen yet: any of it in a browser -- the start of a headless Chrome was refused this maker as it was the first, so `A:\swg3js\port\PASS1-loadtimes.md` holds the method, the commands and wave 1's own split, and no journey of this wave's was measured. Found in the proof over the first maker's half: at 577fb27 three's built WebGL renderer still keys the light probes' count (only its source dropped it), so the WebGL key readers moved to thirteen light counts (`shaderWatch.ts`, `programCensus.ts`) misread the running game's keys while their tests, which read three's source, pass; and in node the WebGL chunks after the game's holds are the base's text in 171 of 180 files, the nine others three's changes on paths the game does not draw, but for the one-level tables and shadow maps now read at level 0, which read the same texel (`docs/lessons/webgpu.md`).

**Pass 1's second wave: the fixer** (held on `claude/pass1-w2` until a browser has seen it, since every headless Chrome start of the wave was refused, then `claude/three-187`, not yet on main). Mended: the card's named timers read the build's answer (`gpuTimer.ts`'s `timerMs` takes the build's bare milliseconds and the src/'s record alike), with `gpuLedger.test.ts` driving three's build classes (its backend timers, its WebGPU pool over a stand-in card, its `GPUTimer`) and the clock's anchors in `threeAnchors.test.ts` read off `build/three.webgpu.js` class by class, the answer's shape pinned; the WebGL key readers put back to fourteen light counts with their captured keys, and `shaderWatch.test.ts` reading three's key order off `build/three.module.js`; six stray files a maker left under `node_modules/three/src/` taken out, so the tree runs the pinned bytes exactly (6,098 files, the same as a fresh `npm ci`); a sweep of every three file a node suite reads or imports out of `src/` against the build found no other difference that matters (rollup's renames and stripped comments aside: the probes in the WebGL renderer, an occlusion map laid on the node material's `getGI` light, the VSM shader's names, none reached by a suite's check or by a material of the game's). Not seen yet: everything in a browser (the start of a Chrome stayed refused, so no patch was proven by measure, `patchApplies` still accepting `188dev` for all five on the node proof; no census, frame report, arrival, walk, journey, WebGL program comparison or picture pair), and the warm-up stays carried rather than rewritten onto three's `compileAsync`, for the reasons maker A gave, until the tab says otherwise.

**Animations from Mixamo** (Animations I, on `claude/animations`, wave 1; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN.md`). Built: a developer's importer (`npm run anims -- import "<folder>"`, `tools/anim/README.md`) that turns the owner's Mixamo FBX files into a clip pack of 1.5 MB committed under `public/anims/mixamo/` and shipped with every build (81 clips: his 77 files and a mirrored twin of four one-sided strafes), so players download nothing more and the FBX files are never committed; a fitter that puts the clips on every two-legged species' skeleton as its rig loads, with the pelvis level (the old way of lining the two rest poses up tipped the hips 32 degrees back) and with no clip ever moving the body; and the brief's "Generic" swaps, for every class: treading water when still in the water, the flying loop adrift on foot in space, the long fall and the flat impact when thrown from a wrecked vehicle (moving, jumping, the postures, the fight, the ability slots, the grapple and the emotes held until the impact has played, then lying prone as before; dying, being stood elsewhere or falling into water while thrown ends it), the jump's start and the falling loop in the air (the floating loop after a flip, a Force jump or a wall move), and a landing by how fast the body came down: none under 3.6 m/s, the short one under 9.8 (every hop: a plain one comes down at 5.7 m/s, a Force user's at 7.4 to 9.6 by how long the tap was, through the game's own movement at 20 to 240 frames a second), the longer one from there up (a Force user's harmless drops from about 2.4 m; a body with no Force is hurt from 1.7 m and lands those in the hard one), the hard one whenever the fall hurt, a held move key ending it after 0.35 s into whatever the body is doing by then. Jedi Academy's own jump, in-air pose and landing stay while blocking or fighting, as the owner asked, and a roll and a Force jump's harmless landing are Jedi Academy's too; a flip, a Force jump and a wall move open with a moment of the plain jump's start (about a tenth of a second), since how long the key will be held is not known as it goes down, which is the owner's to accept or not. Walking out of water plays what it always did on the shore (a moment of Jedi Academy's own landing) and no imported one, and a held blaster's aim correction is held while an imported clip has the arms. Every number of feel is a slider under `anim` in the debug menu (`__debug.anim({ tune })`), `__debug.anim()` reports the pack, each skeleton's fit and the player's pose against the bind pose, `anim({ play })` plays any of the 81 on the player by its id or its file's name, `__debug.files()` lists the pack's files among the session's, and `localStorage['swg.mixamo'] = '0'` and a reload are the game before it, but for one mend that holds either way: a body thrown from a wreck that falls into water, dies or is stood elsewhere no longer lies down prone where it next comes to ground. Seen only in headless tabs of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and thirteen screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\`), never by the owner: a human male and a Wookiee (both Jedi) each trod water with the hips 35.0 to 36.2 degrees off the bind pose, which is the source's own lean and nothing added (the game's own idle stands them 10.8), drifted in the flying loop, jumped into the jump's start and the falling loop, landed with nothing from 0.3 m, the short landing from 1.2 m, the longer one from 2.6 to 9 m and the hard one from 14 m (27 health), each landing ending in the idle with both ankles within 2 mm of where they rest, floated after a Force jump and landed it in Jedi Academy's own landing, kept Jedi Academy's jump behind the block, and thrown from a wrecked speeder fell in the long fall, hit in the impact with the keys held 1.2 s (the body moved nought with W held) and crawled once freed; a human female Bounty Hunter's hop came down at 5.7 m/s into the short landing, and her drop of 4 m, which hurts her where it does not hurt a Jedi (19 health), into the hard one; no program was built in play by any of it, and the ten clips were fitted in 4 to 6 ms inside the rig's load. Its review round's mends (fingertips, a one-off's anchor, the impact's flat landing with no footsteps, a thrown fall ended by a death or by water, the slots held in the impact, no landing on the shore, a landing's footsteps folded, the aim's correction held, the pack's files in `files()`, a landing cut into the run, the impact at a hold of nought, every hop the short landing, `anim({ play })` by a file's name) were driven again there, in numbers and thirteen fresh screenshots (`fix-human-*.png`; the first thirteen are from before it): a Jedi's jump with the key down 33 to 117 ms came down at 7.8 to 9.3 m/s into the short landing every time and from 133 ms was a Force jump into Jedi Academy's own; his drops of 1.2 and 1.6 m landed short, 2.6 and 6 m in the longer one, 14 m in the hard one (27 health), and a Bounty Hunter's 1.2 and 1.6 m short and 2.6 m (7 health), 6 m and 14 m hard; W pressed a tenth of a second into the longer and the hard landing ended each a third of a second after touchdown in the run's own clip; thrown with `impact.lock` at nought the impact played alone with no key held; with every slot, the grapple, an emote, the jump and W held through the hold (1.18 s) the body rose and moved nought; killed in the air and respawned, a Bounty Hunter stood at the spawn and ran at once (as did a Jedi with the switch off, who by 87280ee's code lay prone there: read, not driven), and killed in the impact she came round with the keys free and still prone, which is the game's own rule for a posture; a dive and a walk out played a moment of Jedi Academy's landing on the shore and no imported one; the Bounty Hunter aiming through a hop stood at most 26 degrees off the view (58 before the mend) and through a 4 m drop 26 (42); and with the switch off the pack was asked for nothing and `anim({ play })` said the switch. The programs built in play in those sessions were the world's own (furniture and people streaming in after console teleports, a speeder spawned from the console), none the player's. Not built: the long fall plays only when thrown from a wreck, since nothing in the game yet pushes a player off their feet (the brief's "falling from being pushed" waits for something that does); the pack's walks, runs, strafes, stairs, crouches, climbs and hangs are imported, measured and playable from the console and nothing blends them into the movement (later waves); other players see the tread, the flying loop and the falling loop only through the state already sent, and none of the one-offs; creatures and droids are not fitted. The owner's to judge, all by eye: the pelvis and the stomach on a real body, each clip's look on each species, whether the short landing (which starts with the feet 0.43 m up and brings them down in a quarter of a second) and the longer one (0.13 m up at its trim) read right against a body already on the ground, where the line between the short landing and the longer one belongs (`land.soft`, started at 9.8, just over the fastest a jump that is no Force jump comes down, so that every hop is the short one; at the first cut's 7 and the review round's 8 a Force user's tap of more than a few hundredths of a second landed in the longer one), whether a landing should be heard as a footstep at all, how long the impact should hold the keys, and whether the flying loop is the right float for space. **Wave 2a gave the walk on foot its paces** (on the branch, not yet on main; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W2A.md`). Built: standing on foot the legs play the pack's own walks, jogs, runs and strafes by a table that is the brief's Locomotion list (twenty clips: eight ways by walk, jog and run, the sprint forward, the tired jog and the slow strafe), and the body moves at the class's own speed: the jog with nothing held (2.85 m/s forward), the run with Shift held (4.9), the sprint with Shift let go and pressed again within 0.35 s (6.35, the one run the game had; forward only), the walk with Alt held (1.44; sideways it is the slow strafe, 0.59), a class lower every half second after Shift is let go; backing up and strafing keep the body facing the view, the diagonals' jogs have clips of their own and a walked or run diagonal turns the body 45 degrees with the torso taking half back, except with a blaster up, where a diagonal plays its own jog clip at every pace and the body stays square (`gunSquare`, a rule of ours over the brief's, on); stamina is a third bar (100 points: the sprint spends 12 a second, the run holds, the forward jog gives 4 back, walking, standing and backing up 10, sideways from the jog up nothing and at the walk 10 (`stamina.sideWalk`), the air and the water giving none back while a sprint through a jump still spends, and a seat, a ride, a flight and noclip giving it back as standing does; the walk sideways giving back is the owner's answer of 2026-11-15), and emptied it tires the body to a slower jog (2.38) with no run until three tenths are back; sprinting, a fight's key (the attack, the other attack, the other trigger, the saber's throw, the block) ends the sprint on its press and goes through, the body dropping to the run until a fresh double tap of Shift (`sprintFight` 'ends', the owner's word of 2026-11-15; 'muted', the first rule, left them doing nothing while it lasted and a block capping the pace only while held), and getting on anything ends the sprint; a block or a swing holds the pace to the run on Jedi Academy's own legs (its saber walk up to the jog, its saber run from the run up, its back-pedal backing), a blaster aimed to the jog; two striding clips fade in step from the left foot's landing; every fitted clip is anchored where the game's own idle holds the hips; and other players see the same clip, worked out by their game from the state, heading and speed already sent (no word is new and the relay is as it was). Shift is the `speedUp` action now (run and sprint on foot, the boost on a vehicle and a ship, fast in noclip and the editor) and the walk Alt, held, on foot. Every number is `LOCO_TUNE` (`__debug.loco({ tune })`, sliders under `loco` in the debug menu), `__debug.loco()` reports the class, the clip, the rate, the turn, the stamina, the stride and the peers, and `localStorage['swg.loco'] = '0'` or `loco({ on: false })` is the movement before it (one run at 6.35, Shift or Alt walks, the body turns the way it runs, no stamina row), held line by line against 3432677. Seen only in headless tabs of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and twenty screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\w2a-*.png`), never by the owner: a human male Jedi moved at each class's speed in all eight ways with its clip at 0.93 to 0.96 of its own rate (the run 0.85, the sprint 1.09), faced the view backing and strafing and stood 45 degrees off it on a walked or run diagonal; Shift held ran, let go 0.2 s and pressed sprinted, dropped to the run at 0.5 s and the jog at 1.0 s; the sprint spent 30 points in 2.5 s, emptied it jogged tired at 2.38 and ran again from 30; on flat ground the planted ankle slid 6 to 11 per cent of the ground speed against the travel, 2 to 5 cm a plant, in every way and class but the forward jog (13 per cent the other way, its path 34 cm a plant by the looser measure) and more wherever a slope held the body under its commanded speed (the clip's rate follows the speed commanded, as the old run's did); a human Bounty Hunter kept the body square with a blaster up in every way and class and took the slow strafe aimed; with the switch off the run was 6.35 on the game's own clip, Shift walked at 1.55 and the body turned the way it ran; and on a relay of this build two tabs saw the same legs' clip on both screens in 26 of 26 Jedi cases and 8 of 8 blaster ones, after two faults found there were mended (a body turned by the aim on an aimed diagonal, and a runner on a dune whose travel lay 14 to 20 degrees off its heading). Its review round (33 findings) built the owner's two answers, ended a sprint and its mutes in every branch off the feet, put the stop, the tap window, the legs under a block, the switch flipped on the move, the stamina row's place and the peers' states right, made the frame allocate nothing, eased the rig's steady hold and held the first-person view this side of a wall; in the same tabs afterwards a fighter mounted mid-sprint fired six bolts in a second and a half as its control did, a stop from the sprint took 0.32 s and 0.77 m as with the classes off, an aimed start or stop left the barrel within 7.5 degrees for a few frames where it had swung 15 to 27 and the movement before the wave 8, the first-person view pressed to a wall stood 0.12 m this side of it at every pace where it had stood up to 0.16 m through it, and two tabs on a relay of this build saw the same legs' clip in 32 of 32 Jedi cases and 30 of 30 blaster ones. **Its fix round (2026-11-15, the owner's words "let's reimport the animations to fix them")** mended the planted feet: the importer reads a clip's speed off its stances' settled middles against the ground it is carried on and the game scales a stride by the legs' own lengths (the lesson above), the owner's clips were imported again with only the index's speeds moving (every table clip 2 to 12% faster, not one `.mxa` changed) and no pace's dial moved, so the clips' rates moved instead; `locoSlip.test.ts` plays the clip the game fits between its keys and holds each planted foot within 2% of the ground across its stances' middles on every table clip and three figure sizes (1.85% at worst, the run's right foot; the two feet together 1.0%; the Ithorians' longer thigh 1.5% together and 2.5% on the run's right foot), and in a headless tab of ours, on the fix round's first cut (whose speeds its review then moved by -1 to +1.2%), the planted ankle's slip along the travel went from 8.3, 4.6, 14.6, 10.0, 5.2 and 9.8% back (the walk, the slow strafe, the jog, the run, the sprint, the backward jog, on a human) to 2.4% forward, 0.2 back, 0.1 back, 1.4, 3.6 and 1.0% forward, and on a Bothan woman at 0.72 of the size from 8.2, 4.1, 14.3, 10.5, 1.5 and 10.0% back to 2.5, 1.3, 0.2, 1.9, 2.4 and 1.1% forward (that measure is the ankle alone, where the node test's is the whole foot). A fight's key now ends a sprint (above), seen there with a lit saber, whose swing started on the press's own frame, and a blaster, which fired on it; and walking sideways gives stamina back, the owner's answer. Any of the five keys ends a sprint whatever is in the hands, one that does nothing with it included (the other trigger with a saber, the throw with a gun, a click with an instrument), which is the brief's list read to the letter and the owner's to narrow. Not built (wave 2b and after): the crouched and prone sets, stairs, idle fidgets, turns in place, starts and stops, the creator's gait, a peer's torso and head turned back and a peer's own aimed pose (with a gun their upper body keeps the standing pose of the carry their state names); under a block or a swing at the jog the legs are Jedi Academy's saber walk at 1.43 of its own rate; Force Speed and the lava's pace change a peer's speed and so the class their figure shows; on the grapple's line the pace is the jog, since Shift is the reel there; a friend on a build from before the wave sees its one run and walk whichever way you go. The owner's to judge, all by eye and by hand: every pace's speed and how each clip reads at it (the forward jog's slide above all), the run against the sprint (their clips are nearly one), the Shift timings, how fast stamina spends and returns, the 45-degree turn and the torso's half, the spine's turn under an aimed blaster over these legs (69 degrees against the 34 it had), the 0.2 s fades, whether the stamina bar belongs under the other two, whether a pace's dial should now stand at its clip's own measured speed, and where `gunSquare`, `stamina.air` and `fight.walkUnder` should start. **Its polish (2026-11-15, the owner's first look; `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W2A-POLISH.md`)** built, each a switch whose 'before' is the game as it was: walking sideways plays the walked diagonal's own clip with the body square to the view (`sideWalkClip` 'diagonal'; 'forward' the forward walk turned 90 degrees, 'strafe' the slow strafe); a plain jump floats in the Force jump's float with no start until it falls past 10 m/s, then the falling loop, the rope's air is the float, and a step off a ledge 1.5 m deep or more plays the hop (`airRule`); treading at the surface the drawn figure sits 0.3 m lower (scaled by the figure) and held still under the water it treads (`swim.rule`); Jedi Academy's clips' lower back rebased onto the game's idle (`jkaSpine`, 486 clips on a rig); a gun out of combat plays the pace's whole clip for a pistol and the game's own carry walk and run for the rest (`gunCarry`); the paces start at the owner's speeds (walk 1.66, jog 4, tired 3.5, run 6, sprint 7; back 3 and 4; sideways 1.44, 4 and 6; diagonals 1.66, 4 and 6 forward, 3 and 4 back), the progression's sprint base with them; and each pace's animation speed is a share of its feet-locked rate (`rate.byClass`, 1). Seen only in headless tabs of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\polish-*.png`), never by the owner: a human Jedi walked sideways on the strafe walk square to the view at 0.76 of its rate, on the forward walk turned 90 at 0.86, and on the slow strafe held at the band's 1.6 where it would need 2.42; a plain jump floated near its apex (the falling loop under 'before'), and one taken 6 m up floated until it fell at 10.1 m/s, then fell; treading at the surface the figure was drawn 0.333 m lower, the shoulders at the waterline, and held still on a lake's bed it trod where 'before' swam; the paces moved at 1.66, 4, 6 and 7 m/s with their clips at 0.99, 1.15, 0.94 and 1.11 of their rates, the sprint at 0.94 under a share of 0.85; a Bounty Hunter's pistol under 'full' at the sprint came no nearer the spine's axis than 0.17 m over 90 frames (its standing carry held over the legs, 'split', 0.18), and a carbine's 'original' played the rifle's own run at 0.66 of its rate at the jog and 1.16 at the sprint; no program was built in play for the player. Not built: a two-tab check of what a peer shows (the maker's ports exclude a relay), the rope's float and the ledge hop in a browser (node holds both through the game's own movement), and a pelvis taken per frame under a swing over the imported legs (wave 2b's `swingPelvis` takes the swing's own pelvis over whatever legs, below, measured over Jedi Academy's). The owner's to judge: every one of them by eye, where `fall.floatUntil` and `fall.hopDrop` should stand, how far the treading figure should sink, whether the rebased lower back reads right in a swing, and how much the 'original' carries' feet may slide (43% of the speed on a human's rifle run).

**Animations wave 2b, items A to D** (on `claude/animations`, built on 7424bad; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W2B.md`; items E and F, the slope limit and the swing's lean-back, are another maker's). Built: **crouched** (Ctrl held) has three paces by the keys standing uses and no sprint -- Alt the sneak walk (0.81 m/s), nothing the crouched walk (1.63, what crouching moved at before), Shift the game's own crouched walk (2.2) -- every way but back the pace's forward clip with the body turned toward the travel (45 degrees on a diagonal, 90 sideways) and the torso and head back on the view, backing up Jedi Academy's crouched walk back with the body to the view (0.61, 1.22, 1.65); **prone** (Z) the game's crawl (0.66), the low crawl (0.95) and the running crawl (2.15), the body turning the way it crawls as before, backing up turned round (`proneBack` 'today') or the crawl played backwards with the body to the view ('reverse'), a gun in hand keeping its own prone crawl at the pace's speed; crouched and prone the stamina comes back at the walk's rate on the slower two and the jog's on the quickest. A **gait** (standard, masculine, feminine, heavy, the game's own) chooses the walk's clips only, kept on the record (`gait`, none for the standard), chosen on the creator's and the in-game appearance page (the Walk row, whose pick plays the walk in place on the doll for four seconds) or `loco({ gait })`, and sent in the hello; **WAVE 2B CHANGED THE RELAY**: `server/wire.mjs` passes a hello's `gait` from a closed list, so the relay must be restarted for others to see a gait, and an older relay or build shows the standard. **Walking hurt** (under a quarter of the health) is the injured walk forward, on the forward diagonals and sideways (0.57 m/s, the body turned toward the travel), the jog and up unchanged; other players show it where their health reaches this side. Every rule is a switch whose 'before' is 7424bad's frame trace to the bit (`crouchRule`, `proneRule`, `proneBack`, `injured.rule`, the standard gait), every number a slider under `loco`. Seen only in a headless tab of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\w2b-*.png`), never by the owner: on a human male at full height (scale 1.11) the sneak walk held at the band's 0.6 (it would need 0.28), the crouched walk at 1.09, the game's crouched walk at 1.33, the walk back at 0.79, the low crawl at 1.43, the running crawl at 0.90 and the injured walk at 0.91 of their rates; a gait picked on the appearance page walked on the doll with no program built in play. Not built: a two-tab check of a peer's crouch, gait or hurt walk (node holds each), and nothing of the creator driven in a browser (its two lines are pinned). The owner's to judge: every speed, how far the sneak walk's feet slide at the band's bottom, whether the gaits read as their names, and 'reverse'.

**Animations wave 2b, items E and F** (on `claude/animations`, built on 7424bad beside items A to D; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W2B.md`). Built: **a true slope limit** for the player -- a face steeper than the climb limit (55 degrees, `slope.climb`) is never ground, whether terrain or a model's, building's or object's: standing on one, landing on one or walking onto one, the body slides off it at the world's gravity along the face (`slope.slide` 1, Jedi Academy's own rule; less is a rougher face), playing the falling loop (`slope.fade`), and the fall it slides into is paid for its speed into the ground; a plain jump off such a face is refused (`slopeJump` 'refused', 'allowed' the other), the rope and the water are as they were, and every face up to the limit, stairs, ramps, jumps, rolls and landings are 7424bad's frame for frame (`slopeLimit.test.ts`). **A swing over moving legs leans as it does standing** (`swingPelvis` 'mended'): a Jedi Academy swing riding the upper body stands on its own pelvis, not the legs', so over the back-pedal or a walk it no longer leans up to 33 degrees back and swings over people, and over a run it no longer leans 11 to 15 forward. Each is a switch whose 'before' is 7424bad's trace to the bit (`slopeRule`, `swingPelvis`), every number a slider (the debug menu's `loco`, "The slope"; `anim`, the swing's choice), and `loco()` reports `slope`, `anim()` `swingPelvis` and the pose's `chestLeanDeg` and `reachDeg`. Seen only in a headless tab of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\w2b-slope-*.png`, `w2b-swing-*.png`), never by the owner: on Tatooine near the arrival, the steepest terrain cell (75 degrees) held a body 1.25 m up its face for 1.5 s under 'before' and slid it 3.23 m to the foot under the limit; of 961 drops from 30 m over the buildings round the arrival, 9 that came to rest perched 2 to 8 m up a building's face under 'before' slid to the ground under the limit (12 had the controller's word overturned); a walk up a 40-degree slope was the same to the millimetre under both; and a human Jedi's strong swing chained from a stance, standing, ended its chest 32.3 degrees forward with its reach 74.8 down, backing up 6.6 and 56.5 under 'before' and 34.3 and 74.7 mended, strafing 10.8 and 15.9 against 14.9 and 19.3. Its review round (in node, never driven in a browser since): a body dropped on a steep ridge's peak, a model's or the terrain's, 65, 80 or 89 degrees, on its top or up to 0.25 m off it, slid off where it had perched under both rules, a lip's top still stands frame for frame as before, a slide no longer ends hanging 0.2 to 0.29 m over the floor at its foot, and the rule now casts no ray while a body stands and one each 16 m while it walks on flat ground, where it cast one every frame (`loco()`'s `slope` counts `casts`, `kept` and `edges`). Not built: the fighters' slope (their controller copy has the same fault and creeps 0.55 to 1.62 m in 2 s down a 65 to 89 degree face; their movement is the AI handoff's), the people's (dynamic bodies, not measured), a body falling straight (not sliding) into the crease where a steep face meets the floor, which can still come to rest leaning on the face up to 0.32 m over the floor (42 of 192 such drops in node, 165 under 'before'), and a side-on picture of a swing (the shots are the chase view). The owner's to judge: where the climb limit should stand, how fast a slide goes and whether a rough face should hold a little, whether jumping off a steep face should be allowed, and whether a swing over the legs now reads as it should.

**Animations wave 3: the feet, stairs and the crease** (on `claude/animations`, built on 23d7898; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W3.md`, the owner's brief's lines 19, 52 and 146). Built: **the feet on what is under them** -- standing on its feet (still, every pace, crouched, kneeling, Jedi Academy's legs, a gun's carry; never the air, the water, prone, riding, the rope, dead, flying, adrift, the boots' surface or a whole-body special move), each planted foot of the player's figure, and of every other player's within `ik.peerReach` (40 m) of the camera, stands on its ground: the leg bent by a two-bone solve, the foot pitched and rolled to its slope (`ik.pitchMost` 35, `rollMost` 20 degrees), on a step's edge the heel, the ball and the toe's tip each on their own ground with the toe bent up to `toeMost` (30) and never down over the edge, a foot over a drop drawn in onto the edge (`edgePull` 0.25 m), the pelvis lowered by what the lower foot needs (`pelvisMost` 0.4 m under the capsule's feet) and back up over `ease` (0.08 s), each foot's planting and any sudden change of its ground eased in over the same, a foot not planted carried down with the pelvis in the clip's own leg, the first-person eye held where the head would be with no drop (`ik.eye` 'steady'). **Stairs** are found by a rule from the ground's heights along the travel, never a mesh's name, and walked forward up and down by the pack's four stair clips (`stairs.ways` 'forward'), at their measured strides (on the human male the up walk's feet go 0.365 m/s, the up run's 0.749, the down walk's 0.485, the down run's 1.119, where the index said 0.387, 0.754, 0.218 and 0.493); the drawn body's height eases onto the capsule's over every step it takes, stairs or a kerb (`stairs.ease` 0.12 s, at most `lagMost` 0.5 m behind), the chase camera following it (`stairs.camera` 'smooth'). **The crease**: a fall into where a face too steep to stand on meets the floor ends on the floor (`slopeCrease` 'floor'). Each is a switch whose 'before' is 23d7898's trace to the bit (`ik.rule`, `stairs.rule`, `slopeCrease`), every number a slider under `loco`, and `__debug.loco()` reports `feet`, `stairs`, `crease` and each peer's feet and stairs (`loco({ side })` swings the chase camera round the figure). In node: on faces of 20 to 50 degrees, terrain and trimesh, up and across, every sole on the face within a millimetre (the 50-degree face faced up, past the pitch's 35, leaves the heel 48 mm over it); beside steps of 0.15 to 0.4 m both soles on their ground; on staircases of 0.15, 0.25 and 0.4 m risers walked and run by the clips and by the game's own movement at 60 and 144 frames a second every planted sole on its tread within a centimetre of where it is put (its ground, less what of a sudden change of it is still being let in, which settles within 0.3 s), but for a few foot-frames of the 0.4 m risers at 144 fps the pelvis's most leaves short; the rule held stairs up and down over three of the game's own buildings' flights (a theatre's, a guard house's, a starport's steps) and never a ramp of 5 to 50 degrees, terraces, a single kerb, a kerb's lip or bumps; 192 of 192 drops into a crease end on the floor (42 hung 0.08 to 0.32 m up under 'before'); and standing in the rest pose the feet cast four rays once and none after, walking a staircase 0.77 a frame at 60 fps, the frame making nothing but the engine's own answers. Seen only in headless tabs of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and twenty screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\w3-*.png`), never by the owner: on a 29.6-degree slope on Naboo a human Jedi's soles stood from 8 cm over to 9 cm in the ground under 'before' and within 15 mm of it under the rule (the pelvis 0.054 m down), crouched from 24 cm over to 19 cm in against 9 mm; on Theed's bridge stairs (a prop, risers of 0.24 m), walked and run up and down, the stairs held for the whole flight going down and 147 of 159 frames going up, the drawn body easing up to 0.47 m behind the capsule running up and held at its 0.5 m running down, every planted sole on its ground within 6 mm, but one frame where the report's ray from over a toe tip at a riser's foot met the tread above and read it 0.24 m in, where under 'before' soles stood up to 0.38 m in the steps and 1.4 m over them; at the stairs' top platform's edge a foot over the drop was drawn 0.25 m in onto the edge; and on a relay of this build a second tab saw the first's figure on those stairs (put down through the server's props) on the stair clips with both feet planted in every sample and its pelvis lowered up to 0.33 m. Not built: the creatures', the fighters' and the people's feet (the AI pass's), stairs on the diagonals by default, and the stairs' pace: the stair clips' feet go far slower than every pace (at the walk's 1.66 m/s the up walk would need 4.55 of its rate, held at the band's 1.6), so they slide on the treads at `stairs.pace` 1, and 0.35 to 0.47 would keep them still. The owner's to judge, all by eye: every dial, whether the stairs should slow the pace, how a pelvis dropped 0.9 m on a run down a flight (the drawn body held 0.5 m over the capsule) reads, the toe's bend, the edge pull, and the steady first-person eye. Its review round mended, in node: the knee bending backward and swinging 0.23 m out of the clip's leg plane wherever a nearly straight leg's target moved forward (walking up a 20-degree hill or onto a kerb; its pole now the posed limb's own, `twoBone.ts`, and over slopes of 10 to 30 degrees and kerbs of 0.2 and 0.35 m at 30, 60 and 144 fps no knee backward and none off the clip's plane past its ankle); the feet and the drawn body popping by a riser in one frame (each foot's share now ramps in over `ik.ease`, a sudden change of a planted foot's ground is let in over it while what its clip does is followed as it stands, and the pelvis goes down with that eased need: a foot slid off a 0.25 m step moves no more a frame than that ease gives, and walking onto and off kerbs the pelvis's drop moves at most 7.7 m/s past the capsule's own at every frame rate, where at the ease's nought it moves 58 m/s at 144); a swinging foot left at the clip's height over a pelvis dropped 0.5 m, its ankle over its hip (a foot not planted now rides down with the pelvis in the clip's own leg); and an idle swaying the feet casting rays for good (each probe slot keeps four answers: in node an idle swaying them a centimetre casts 14 rays over its first sway and none after; standing with the species' own idle at Naboo's arrival, in a headless tab of ours, 40 rays over the first half minute and none over the 50 s after, which is the standing cost, not none). Two things it says that the wave did not: on flat ground the whole figure now stands 3.9 cm lower than at 23d7898, the character controller's 0.04 m hover taken out by the feet (under `ik.rule` 'before' every sole stands 0.039 m over the floor); and the two descending stair clips are not loops (the index marks them one-offs; played round, both ankles jump 0.30 to 0.50 m at every wrap), so a planted foot stays on its tread through the jump while a swinging one shows it: a cycle window or a mirrored half is a choice for a later wave.

**Animations wave 4, item A: hanging from a ledge** (on `claude/animations`, built on e2b9776; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W4.md`, the owner's brief's lines 157 to 159; item B, climbing a wall face and the hands bracing on walls, is maker B's, who reads the hang's state off `Player.climbHolds` and `Player.ledge`, spends stamina through `player.loco.stamina.step`, adds its groups to `CLIMB_TUNE` beside `CLIMB_RANGES`/`CLIMB_CHOICES` and `CLIMB_GROUPS`, and places a hand through `LimbPlace.setHand` and `place`). Built: a lip within the hands' reach is grabbed by a jump with W at it, by falling past it moving toward it (no faster than `grabFall` 12 m/s), or by crouch-walking off a top a hang deep, and hung from **braced** (the wall under the lip within the feet's reach, feet on it) or **free** (an overhang, arms straight), on the twenty-four of the owner's parkour clips the table names (`LEDGE_ROLES`; the twins set aside are measured and listed); A and D shimmy at the clip's own pace along the lip's own edge (the fix round's, the owner's additions while the wave ran: a `Rail` of samples a 0.1 m apart followed round outer and inner corners and along curves, each hand gripping the lip under it at its own height, the body hung from the two hands' middle and turned to face their faces' mean, and on a sloping lip by `lipTilt` 0.5 of its slope), stopping a hand short of where no lip goes on, a part steeper than `lipSlope` (35 degrees), or where the body's capsule would meet something; Space with A or D hops to the nearest other lip that way (`hopSide`, `hopUp` 1.5 m, their sliders to 8: straight on across a gap, a face turned up to 60 degrees, or a block's near side facing back, on another face, the body turning to it as it goes), its way checked for the capsule's room first (straight, else bent out from the faces or up over what stands in its line, else refused in words), Space with W hops braced up the same face, Space or W climbs up onto the top (its capsule carried out clear of the face, up past the lip and in over the top crouched, every step of it clear or the climb refused in words; the figure drawn on the climb's own straight line as before), the crouch key lets go, S with Space jumps off backward; hanging drains stamina (3, 5 shimmying, 12 a hop) and an empty pool lets go with no grab until it is back; a grab ends a fall with no harm; the fight, the postures, the saber's switch, the use key, the emotes and the class switch are muted while hanging (`LEDGE_MUTES`; `playEmote` refuses a climb as well), the grapple refuses in words, and a grapple haul that ends under a lip it cannot go over hangs braced there with the rope let go (`HAUL_STALL` 0.3 s against the face, or the haul's own end);
