the hands are placed on the lip and the braced feet on the wall by a two-bone solve, the body hung from the hands; other players draw the hang's eight rig states (`hangBraced` to `climbUpFree`, within the relay's 32 characters) lined up by the same table, their hands on a lip their own game finds under them within `ik.peerReach` (round its corners and up its slope, tilted as the hanger's own); a browser built before the wave draws a player hanging or climbing standing idle at the hang's place, in the air beside the wall (its `STATES.has(s) ? s : 'idle'`). `climb.ledges` 'before' is e2b9776's body to the bit (`wave4DayOne.test.ts` against that commit's harness and grapple out of git, and its pack's other 44 clips), every number a slider under `climb` and `__debug.climb` the helper. In node, at 30, 60 and 144 fps: the same grab lands the body at the same place at every rate, a running jump at the same lip too; a lip 3.4 m up is not grabbed, a crouch never grabs, a fall at 20 m/s past a lip goes past; a braced and a free hang, a shimmy at the clip's pace to within 8 mm of a lip's end, an inner corner, hops sideways and up, a climb onto a top at every rate, the climb refused under a low ceiling and on a steep top, a jump off landing 2.3 m back, the pool letting go and refusing a regrab; every hand wholly placed within a centimetre of the lip's line frame by frame through a grab's end, a hang, a shimmy and a hop's landing (worst 0.02 mm), every braced foot's ball 4 cm off the wall within 5 mm, no elbow or knee off the clip's own bend, a peer's figure's hands on the lip within 0.02 mm; and a steady hang's frame making nothing (standing and in the air the sense casts 0 and 1 rays a frame, a still hang 0, a shimmy 4 since the fix round, every cast the engine's own bytes). The fix round's own, in node at 30, 60 and 144 fps (`ledgeCorners.test.ts`, and a corner, a 34-degree lip and a figure 1.22 times the human male's in `ledgeHands.test.ts`): round an outer and an inner corner and along curves of 1.2 and 3 m radius with every hand on the lip under it within a centimetre (worst 0.01 mm) and the capsule never in a wall; a lip at 20 and 34 degrees shimmied up and down whole and one at 40 not, the hands within a centimetre of the lip under each and the body turned by half the slope; a crate at the knees and a flush post rising past the lip stopping the shimmy; a hop arcing over a pillar in its gap, one to a block's near side on another face, one 5 m off at a reach of 6; the climb's capsule never in the wall, nor under a roof 1.25 m over the top; a bigger figure's foot past a wall's end eased off it. Seen only in a headless tab of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and nine screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\w4-*.png`), never by the owner: on Tatooine beside Bestine, on a slab of the props sunk to stand 2.36 m and a diner counter raised to stand 2.83 m, a human male Jedi caught the slab's lip braced (hands 1 and 1, the wall's feet 1) and the counter's free, shimmied 0.53 m in a second, hopped along a gap in the slab's face, and climbed up onto the top standing; the 19 programs built in play were the town's own pieces the teleports streamed in, none of the hang's. Not built: ledges for the creatures, fighters and people, a hang on anything that moves (a vehicle, a ship's rooms, a peer's ship), a peer's one-offs (their figure plays the hang's loops and the climb's clip, the idle through a hop), a hop round a corner on one lip going on (the shimmy's), a free hang's hop up (no clip), and the hop's leave and land shares (a sixth of the clip each, ours); the fix round's corners, slopes and hops have been driven in node only, never in a browser. The owner's to judge, all by eye: every dial, whether a Jedi's tap jump should grab or its Force jump carry it past, how the crossfade into a hang reads with the body hung from the hands, whether crouch-walking off a top should lower into a hang at all, how a body reads going round a corner, how much of a slope it should lean with (`lipTilt`), and whether a long hop should take longer than its clip (today it covers any distance in the clip's own time).

**Animations wave 4, item B: climbing a wall, and the hands bracing** (on `claude/animations`, built on e2b9776 beside item A; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-W4.md`, the owner's brief's lines 19 and 157 to 159: "a Breath of the Wild-like wall climbing system that takes stamina", and the hands against walls, in tight spots, in crowds and in space). Built: **W held into a face too steep to stand on** (steeper than the slope's 55-degree climb limit, no more than a hair past upright, reaching past the raised hands; the fix round's: the chest's and the hands' rays reach as far as a face at that limit stands there, so every face from 55.5 degrees starts alike at every rate, where 55 to 61 had been neither walked nor climbed) and cut short there for `wallStart` (0.25 s) **climbs it**, on the ground or in the air (but never in the air while Jedi Academy's own rebound off a wall is open to the body, `JkaMovement.airWallMoveOpen`: Force Jump 3, no special under way, its own guard, so Space rebounds there as before): W up at 1 m/s along the face, S down at 1.2 (standing on walkable ground at its foot), A and D sideways at 0.8, on the owner's two Mixamo climbing clips (`climbing_up_wall`, `climbing_down_wall`, fitted as loops with their climb taken out) played at the rate that keeps their planted hands still, sideways on the up clip slowed, held still on the clip stopped; a face leaning back is climbed leant into it; reaching a lip the ledges take the body as a braced hang at its own place under it and W still held climbs on up onto the top (the hang's own climb, the top-out the clips have not got); the crouch key lets go, Space jumps off backward (the ledges' jump-off clip and turn); climbing costs 8 stamina a second and holding on 4, and an empty pool lets go in words; a bump a few centimetres proud is climbed past, and where something stands out over the face (an eave, a roof) the climb stops under it and says "No way on up here." once. The figure is drawn with its clip's own wall on the face (`clipWall`, `clipLift`, the table `WALL_FACTS` measured on the human male), its hands each on the face by a kept ray and anchored where a hand reaching for its next hold would stand short, its feet on the face where the clip plants them (`WALL_PLANT`); other players' figures on a wall play the same clips at the same pace, leant into the face this side finds in front of them, their hands and feet placed within `ik.peerReach`. **The hands brace** (`src/player/brace.ts`): moving into a wall within reach of the shoulders (three rays at the shoulders' height, only while moving or pushing, and with no face felt at the last look and the move not cut short only every `WALL_LOOK` 0.25 s: the fix round's, a walk in the open cast three a frame) the hand on that side rests its palm on it, turned flat against it, by how near and how hard; head on, both hands; in a passage narrower than `brace.tight` (1.4 m) both; passing a player, a person or a creature not fighting you, the hand on their side rests on them; adrift in space within `brace.spaceReach` (1.2 m) of a surface, both hands hold it and step along hand over hand (moving nothing); a hand holding anything never braces, and nothing does while fighting, aiming, swinging, emoting, hanging, climbing, riding or swimming; other players' figures brace by the same rule within 40 m. Every number is a slider under `climb` ("Wall climbing", "The hands bracing"), `climb({ wall: false })` and `climb({ brace: false })` the body and arms as they were (e2b9776's body to the bit, `wallDayOne.test.ts`), and `climb()` reports the wall (`stall`: why a move was refused; `lip`: what the last look for one found) and the bracing. In node at 30, 60 and 144 fps: up an upright face and a 70-degree one at the speed along the face, the capsule 0.37 m off it, handed over to the hang at one place at every rate and topped out; never an overhang, a walkable slope, a wall under the hands, a tap or a run nudged in at 45 degrees; sideways, down onto the ground, the crouch's let-go, Space's jump off 1 m back, the drain at 8 and 4 a second and the tired let-go; a 63-degree slab's 27-degree lean eased out through the hang; the stall under a roof said once; a band 8 cm proud climbed past; every wholly placed hand a palm off the face within a centimetre (worst 0.00 mm) and every planted foot's ball within 6.2 mm, no elbow or knee off the clip's side; a braced hand's grip on its target and its palm flat; a peer's hands on the face and every offset taken back off; the bracing's walls, passage, head on, crowd, held hands, busy and space rules; and the frame making nothing (on the wall still and bracing still 0 rays and 0.04 bytes a frame, climbing 7.0 to 7.4 rays a frame, bracing along a wall 3 and walking in the open 0.18 at 60 fps, each the engine's own bytes; 17 of 18 mutations caught, the 18th guarded twice); and the fix round's faces of 56 to 62 degrees climbed alike at every rate, and a Force 3 body falling beside a wall at 144 fps rebounding off it as before (at 30 and 60 fps the harness's body sticks to the wall on the ground's verdict before and after: the old port's). Seen only in a headless tab of ours on Direct3D 11, in numbers and seven screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\w4-wall-*.png`, `w4-brace-wall.png`), never by the owner: near Bestine a human male Jedi climbed a Corellian city wall prop (5 m) from the press in 25 frames, handed over at its lip and topped out, climbed the starport's front 2.5 m, sideways 1.07 m in 40 frames and down 1.2 m in 30, stalled under the starport's and the cantina's eaves with the words, and braced a hand on the city wall strafing into it (share 0.92 to 0.99); the 10 programs built in play were the wall prop's and a town Jawa's eye, none of the climb's. Not built: climbing for the creatures, fighters and people; climbing anything that moves (a vehicle, a ship's rooms, a peer's ship); a sideways or a still clip of its own (a Mixamo "climbing left/right" pair would take the sideways climb's place); onto a top too steep to stand on there is no lip by the ledges' rule, so a face that curves over into such a slope (the starport's arch) is climbed to under the curve and stops; and in space the hands move nothing (pulling yourself along would be a rule of play). The owner's to judge, all by eye: every dial, how a climb reads at its speeds, whether the lean into a slab looks right, whether a run nudged into a wall should climb (today it must be within about 37 degrees of straight in, the move's own cut-short test), and how much the hands reaching for walls and people help or distract.

**Animations: the stairs wave** (on `claude/animations`, built on 9c18665; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-STAIRS.md`, the owner's 2026-11-15). Built: the hips ride a steady line under the body (`ik.hips: 'steady'`) instead of dropping for whichever foot is lower, so a walk or jog up or down a 10 to 30 degree slope bobs about its line what it bobs on flat ground (within 1 cm at 30, 60 and 144 fps, where wave 3's rule added up to 26 cm), and a kerb or a flight is climbed with no pop; the stair clips are trimmed to a loop and the steps down mirrored for the other foot (`stairs.cycle`, `stairs.mirror`); and one step a stair (`stairs.stepRule`): the sampler measures each flight's tread, riser and next riser, the step takes n treads a step (`stairs.perStep` 'auto'), goes at the clip's own pace for these stairs eased toward the class's (`stairs.pace` 0.25, a new meaning) inside a rate band, plays the clip by the distance gone with every footfall steered onto the middle of a tread, reaches the feet by the stride n treads ask, lifts a swinging foot over each nosing and holds a planted foot where its sole stands (`stairs.pin`). Other players take the same step from the stairs measured under their figures; nothing of it crosses the wire, so a browser built before the wave draws every player, this build's too, by wave 3's rule, and this build draws a player on an older build by the wave's. Every number is a dial under `loco` (`src/anim/locoDials.ts`), the console's `loco()` says the stairs measured, the step and the hips' line, and the README's "The stairs wave" says what a flight measures. Seen only in node at 30, 60 and 144 fps over four flights and in a headless tab of our own on a placed bridge stair in Naboo's Theed (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\ws-*.png`), never by the owner: walking up or down, every footfall lands on a tread, none over a nosing, no planted foot slides over 2 cm mid-flight, and walked onto and off a flight at 60 and 144 fps none over a nosing and none sliding over 3 cm; no planted foot floats over 1 cm on flat ground or a slope; running lands every footfall n treads on from the last. Its review round (in node, and never driven in a browser since) mended a heel strike floating 2 to 3 cm, a foot sliding as a flight was taken on and left, the walk at the top skimming its planted foot along the floor, a run landing three treads and one, a pop of the hips and 8 cm of sinking at 144 fps, a swinging foot sinking into a downhill slope and 40 bytes a frame on stairs. Left: a run's own first stance onto a flight and last off it miss (8 to 14 cm), the first foot after a long landing stands over its tread for most of a second, and a body can stop against a riser for up to 2 s on some flights (the character controller's autostep wants 0.2 m of tread beyond a step; a change for all movement, the owner's). Not built: diagonal, sideways and spiral stairs, ladders, the creatures and people on stairs. How the trimmed and mirrored clips look, how fast the stairs are taken (the clip's own pace is slower than the class's: a walk up 0.40 m/s where the class asks 1.66), whether a run should take two treads a step, and every dial are the owner's.

**Animations: Stairs II** (on `claude/animations`, built on 33d3e67; the brief is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-STAIRS2.md`, the owner's of 2026-11-16). Built: **the step-up at every frame rate** (`src/player/stepUp.ts`, `step.rule` 'mended', the lesson above): the collider put where the body is before every move on the ground, Jedi Academy's own step-slide where the controller's step falls short, and a riser over the step's 0.5 m a wall at every rate; in node on the player's own movement, risers of 0.21 to 0.48 m are climbed at a walk, a jog and a run at 30, 60, 144 and 240 frames a second with no stall over 0.03 s, each costing the same over the floor alone to 3% at every rate, where under 'before' a walk up 0.32 m risers at 144 took 20.6 s with a 1.3 s stall and 0.42 m stopped at every rate. **The stair clips only on stairs**: `stairs.riserMax` 0.422 (twice the base flight's riser, the millimetre over) and a new `stairs.treadMax` 0.79 (two and a half times its tread), a riser or a tread over them walked with the plain classes and stepped up as a kerb. **The clip ends at the last step** (`stairs.hold` 'ahead'): held only while a riser of the way stands ahead within `treadMax` (`StairsAhead`, `StairsHold.stepAhead`), handed to the walk at the second footfall of the clip's that lands past the last riser's reach, judged by where the foot lands and never where the next flight would be taken on again by the next footfall (`StairEnd`: 0.14 to 0.54 m past it at a walk and 0.52 to 0.86 m at a run over the testbeds, within a frame's travel of each other from 30 to 240 frames a second, where 33d3e67's went on 1.3 m; a run keeps the clip across a 1 m landing, a walk walks it, alike at every rate), each foot's first stance under the walk held by `stairs.pin`, and taken on `stairs.takeOn` (0.5 m) before a flight's first riser or one of its treads where that is further, so the clip is on before the capsule meets the riser (taken on within one tread alone, a walk's planted foot slid back 13 cm as the capsule climbed a riser off a landing); `stairs.look` 2.4 so a flight of the longest treads is read in time. **Going down the whole body is mirrored** by default (`stairs.mirror` 'whole', the owner's). Other players take the same rules from the stairs measured under their figures (`peerLoco`'s `StairEnd`, `stairFrameOf`'s `stairEnding`) and nothing new crosses the wire. Every switch and dial is under `loco` in the debug menu (`step.rule` beside the slopes'), and `__debug.loco()` carries `stepUp` and the stairs' `hold`, `ending`, `aheadM` and `refused`. Its review round mended a riser over 0.5 m met at an angle being climbed (0.505 to 0.6 m risers are walls now met square and 30 to 75 degrees off it at every pace and rate, the body never 0.11 m up, and 0.49 m is still climbed there), the Force wall rebound lost at 144 and 240 under the mend (`StepUp.reach`), the hand-over giving way at a different footfall rate by rate and flickering on a landing at some rates, treads just over `treadMax` flickering as the lattice slid (`placeEdges`), the step's rays cast where no face stood in the way (26 to 15 a second on the base flight at 144), and 32 bytes a frame in the hand-over; every hand-over of it is pinned in `grappleWiring.test.ts` and `stairsWiring.test.ts`, and each rule is held by a mutation. Left: the walk's first stance after a hand-over slides 3 to 5 cm and its first toe-off stands a heel up to 3 cm for a frame or two (33d3e67's hand-over, 1.3 m on, slid 5 to 21 cm); the hand-over comes a frame after its footfall, so at a run it lands 0.12 m further on at 30 frames a second than at 240; the first foot onto a flight after a long landing still stands over its tread a moment; risers whose faces lean are slopes to the slope rule and still stall a walk at every rate under either rule (a rule of wave 2b's, not changed); a Jedi pressed against a wall in the air at 30 and 60 clings as before; the fighters stall on stairs as the player did (`StepUp.walk` is their mend, not taken); pressing into a riser between 0.5 and 0.54 m asks the controller twice a frame; and the grapple's cliff scene, fired along a heightfield's own cell line, wraps the rope round the ridge many times at 144 and over from the true height, the rope's fault (`grapple.test.ts` holds the scene under 'before' and runs it off the line under the mend). How the end of a flight and the mirrored way down look, and every dial, are the owner's.

**Animations: Climbing II, phase 1** (on `claude/animations`, built on c88f890; the design is `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`, the owner's of 2026-11-16). Built: **a test course and its harness, and nothing of the game's rules moved**. One layout (`src/anim/climbCourse.ts`, pure: 100 pieces in twelve rows, each in its own frame with its solids, the lips it means, where a body starts and the numbers it tests: braced and free lips at 1.2 to 3.6 m and at today's two jump reaches and 0.25 m past each, outer and inner corners, bends of 30 to 60 degrees both ways, curves of 3 m in 10-degree steps, lips sloping 15, 25, 35 and 38 degrees, steps of 0.2 to 0.6 m, hop gaps sideways, up, down and diagonally from 1 to 6 m, corridors behind of 1.5 to 4 m, walls of 3, 6 and 10 m and an 8 m edge, bars, a rope and poles over pits, fourteen obstacles with 15 m of run-up, a free run), one builder (`src/anim/climbCourseBuild.ts`: the solids as bodiless fixed colliders in the exterior group and one triangle list), the game's `__debug.climbTest()` (behind a short curtain, so nothing is built in play; taken down by `{ clear: true }` and by `leaveTracks` on a travel, the select screen and a jump), and the harness (`tools/swg/tests/climbCourseBody.ts`) that drives the player's own movement, ledges and wall climb through every piece at 30, 60, 144 and 240 frames a second, whose every frame `climbToday.test.ts` holds to a digest recorded at c88f890 (phase 2's switches at 'before' must give it again). What today does there, measured: the jump's reach off the course's floor slab differs by frame rate (standing 3.10, 3.24, 3.25, 3.25 m; running 3.10, 3.15, 3.25, 3.26; off the terrain with no slab the running reach at 30 and 144 is a centimetre higher, which is the ground's and no change), and so do a 1.8 m lip (grabbed only at 144 and 240), the corridor of 1.5 m (caught only at 240) and a 1.3 m obstacle jumped (over only at 144 and 240); W held at a face in the air starts the wall climb, which reaches lips well past a jump's; a free lip is grabbed only at 2.4 m; any step in a lip stops the shimmy, and stepping down 0.35 m or more the leading hand goes round the step's side; a lip sloping 35 degrees is refused at its foot; a hop reaches 1.0 m sideways or diagonally and 1.0 m up, never 1.5; nothing hops or drops down onto a lower lip; the jump off backward catches a wall behind at 2 to 3 m; walls of every height, the 0.2 m edge among them, are climbed and stood on; the wall climb takes a 0.04 m rope and a 0.08 m pole; a bar is grabbed as a free lip; the free run goes through at every rate. How the course looks is the owner's to judge.

**Animations: Climbing II, phase 2** (on `claude/animations`, built on 1e7f577 by two makers; the brief is the last part of `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`). Built, every change a switch under `climb` whose 'before' is phase 1's climbing (`CLIMB_TWO_BEFORE`, `climbTwoBefore()`; with all of them at 'before' the course's trace is c88f890's to the bit, `climbToday.test.ts`): **E (Use) grabs** the nearest lip in reach or starts the wall climb, on the ground too, first while falling with something to climb in reach (the bar's own test, so a falling press beside a speeder with nothing to climb mounts it) and otherwise only where nothing else in the use key's one dispatch took the press, and the action bar offers "climb" (`grab`); **W at a ledge hops up** where there is a lip above (`wUp`); **Space with nothing to hop to** jumps a little off the wall (`spaceOff`); **hops search a band** up, down and either side, near and far (`hops`); a **stepped lip** up to 0.5 m is shimmied hand by hand (`steps`); on a wall **A or D alone plays the braced shimmy clips** (`wallSide`) and **every hand and foot is held on its hold until its next reach, in turn one limb at a time** (`wallLimbs`, `src/player/climbHolds.ts`), a limb carried past `holdMost` or a foot whose hold has gone past its leg's reach reaching at once (about six reaches in ten at the shipped most, straight up and on a diagonal alike, so two or more limbs are often between their holds at once); a **step taller than 0.5 m is the lip's end**, up and down alike (`steps`: down, where just past the lip's end a lower top stands within the hang's own depth, which 'before' went round the side of); **braced, the view turned past 100 degrees** turns the spine toward it, lifts the leading hand into its hop clip's coil reaching toward the view while the other stays on the lip, and lets Space hop to a lip behind (`lookAway`); and four faults phase 1 measured are mended (`corners`, `thinTop`, `slopeLimit`, `grabSweep`). Every number of feel is a slider under `climb`. On the course at the shipped settings (`climbAfter.test.ts`): every braced lip from 1.8 to 3.0 m is grabbed standing at 0.68 to 0.70 s at every rate (1.8 m was only at 144 and 240), and E takes the higher ones by the wall climb; hops reach 1.5 m sideways, up, down and diagonally (2.0 m diagonally) at every rate, where they reached 1.0 m and never down; steps of 0.2, 0.35 and 0.5 m are crossed both ways; the 35-degree lip is shimmied to its end; the turn at a corner's end is the same at every rate; the 8 m wall's 0.2 m edge is hung from, never stood on; the 1.8 m lip, the bars and the corridors behind are caught alike at every rate; looking back and Space reaches a lip behind at 1.5 to 3.5 m and jumps off at 4. Left: the jump's height still differs by frame rate (the movement's own integration, so at 30 fps the wall climb takes the standing-reach lip); the 1.5 m corridor behind is landed on by S and Space (the movement's step-up lifts the capsule over the box's edge on the way up); the free lips over a back wall are never grabbed and E there starts the wall climb under the plate, which stalls in words. None of it has been seen by eye: how the look-away's reach and the wall's held limbs look are the owner's.

**Animations: Climbing II, the feedback round** (on `claude/animations`, built on 12ae8ad by two makers; the brief is the "Feedback round brief" in `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`, the owner's after playing). Built, every change a switch whose other setting is phase 2's (`CLIMB_FEEDBACK_BEFORE` under `climb`, `MOVE_BEFORE` under the new `move`; `climbFeedbackBefore()` sets both, so climbToday's and climbAfter's digests hold): **the grab lets go sooner** (`grabFree`: from `grabFreeAt`, 0.35 of its clip, the hang takes a key, a key pressed earlier kept); **"No room to climb up here." says why** (`roomSay`: the test that failed, what it met and how far, `climb({ room: true })`); **a climb up that nearly fits is climbed short** (`roomRule`: nearer the lip, crouched, lower, or with a brush of `roomBrush`, the course's `room-*` row); **prone lies along the ground** (`move.proneShape` 'lying', `src/player/bodyShape.ts`): a capsule of `lyingRadius` (0.2 m, where the brief started 0.22) as long as the figure (1.8 m), turned with the heading and tipped along the ground under it, so under anything of 0.48 m or more, the brief's 0.5 m slab at every rate; a turn into something still slid out of it, tried by halves, or refused, lying down and getting up refused in words where the body would not fit (and lying down in water deeper than twice the radius or with the rope out); every reader of the worn capsule reads it (the controller, the camera's block, the water, the ragdoll's start, `PlayerTarget.capsule`'s middle and `reachToward`, other players' sensors from the rig state they already send); and **walking under something low ducks without a key** (`move.duck` 'on'): what stands still over the feet and `duckAhead` (0.6 m) past the front along the travel sets the capsule's height down to `duckMost` (0.75), eased at `duckRate` with a lead so it is low enough when it arrives, never grown off the feet past the room over them (a jump or a step off an edge under a slab), the path ahead asked once a quarter metre walking straight, the crouch blended in and the spine and neck bent past it (`duckBend`, `duckNeck`, laid after the mixer and put back), the first-person eye following the head, the crouch key ducking further; never in the air, swimming, climbing, riding, prone, kneeling, on the rope, sliding, in noclip or with the placing hand full. On the round's harness at 30, 60, 144 and 240 (`bodyShape.test.ts`; the course's digests at the shipped settings and with the body's switches at their other setting in `climbFeedbackAfter.test.ts`; the crouch, stairs, beams and a crouched jump with them at their other setting held to `12ae8ad`'s own harness out of git in `bodyShapeToday.test.ts`): a walk and a run pass under beams 1.5, 1.3, 1.1 and 0.9 m up with no key, ducked within 0.009 m alike at every rate; crouched by the key the body ducks to 0.84 m in a 0.9 m tunnel; a jump and a step off an edge under a slab keep the capsule out of it; a 30-degree closed ramp, a 25-degree heightfield and 0.17 m stairs are walked and run with no duck; a lying body crawls under the 0.5 m slab a crouch cannot, onto 0.1 and 0.15 m steps never into them, lies on 8 and 20-degree hillsides 0.04 m off them, and is refused getting up under a slab. Left: between twice the lying radius plus 0.04 and plus 0.08 (0.44 to 0.48 m at 0.2) the controller squeezes through a gap under its own offset at 30 and 60 and not at 144 and 240, as today's crouched capsule does a hair over its own height (1.05 m); a lying capsule crawling over a crease (the foot of a 20-degree hill) rides up to 0.3 m off the ground until the plane under its feet is the slope; the movement's own `s < 1` stops a body asked for under 1 unit a frame at 240 (a crawl at 0.6 m/s: the harness crawls at the classes' 0.95); the lying capsule stands centred on the feet's point, where the prone clip's drawn body lies; the grappling rope mutes prone (its sync stands the capsule upright); a peer's lying sensor is a human's length; and the duck's pose (the rig's layer and bend) is pinned as text, not run. None of it has been seen by eye: how low a duck looks and whether prone's capsule meets the drawn body are the owner's.

**Animations: Climbing II, the smoothness round** (on `claude/animations`, built on d2d5bc4 by two makers; the brief is the "Smoothness round brief" in `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`, the owner's control design of 2026-11-17). Built, every change a switch whose other setting is the feedback round's (`CLIMB_SMOOTH_BEFORE` under `climb`: `shelves`, `hangKeys`, `diagonals`; `MOVE_SMOOTH_BEFORE` under `move`: `duckPace`; `climbSmoothBefore()` sets all four and `climbFeedbackBefore()` calls it, so climbToday's, climbAfter's, climbFeedbackAfter's and bodyShapeToday's digests hold): **shallow lips are lips** (`shelves` 'grab': a top running back `shelfMin` with `handRoom` over it, whatever stands behind, so a rung, a tier, a sill; the grab's search a column of rays gated by one capsule query, and against one plain face by three more, `flatFace`, so a wall climbed asks four queries a frame where 'before' asks two); **one grammar while hanging** (`hangKeys` 'cue': W where the view looks, A and D their side, S down; a hold `ladderMin` to `ladderMax` away climbed by the key alone, each hand on its hold; a hop further shown only while its key is held, a pull-up, a lean, the reach back, and taken by Space; a tap of the crouch drops to the lip beneath, held lets go fully, S held shows the drop); **a diagonal takes a lip over or under the hands** (`diagonals` 'below': W or S with A or D, a level lip the side key's alone; at a zigzag's top rung 'before' took the wall's own top edge beside it over the rung 1 m down); and **ducking slows you to the height you are ducked to and wears its clips** (`move.duckPace` 'height', `src/player/duckPace.ts` and the roof probe in `bodyShape.ts`: the class's speed eased toward the crouch's at the same class by how far down, the crouch's clips at `crouchAt` with `crouchBand`'s hysteresis, past the crouch eased on toward the crawl's; where the room ahead within `proneAhead` is under `proneUnder` and the way under it stays open at a lying body's height for a lying body's length, the body lies down by itself, and it gets up into a crouch where `riseAt` fits over its feet and nothing that low lies ahead; a prone the key asked for never gets up by itself). On the harness at 30, 60, 144 and 240 (`climbSmooth.test.ts`, `duckPace.test.ts`, and `climbRows.test.ts`, which drives every extra piece end to end with only the owner's keys and holds their whole trace, digest 22b33037...): the ladders up rung by rung and down, the zigzags by W or S with a side, the shelves up by the cue (or W onto each top and a jump with E where a tier is past `hopUp`) and down a crouch tap a tier, the chimney face to face and onto its top, the tower's line to its platform with `hopSide` 2.5 and `hopDown` 2; a run under a roof lowering by steps at 3.4, 2.9 and 2.1 m/s where the open run is 6.35, lying down 1.5 m before a 0.55 m slab at every rate, crawling under it and standing again, and a run straight into a 0.6 m slab lying down within one frame's travel at 30 of one place at every rate (the probe reaches the frame's own travel, `roofLead`, and the path box looks as far as the probe); never lying down with the rope out or in water deeper than it lies (Z's own refusals, unspoken); none lying down at a wall, a step, stairs, a ramp, a hill or a table with a wall behind it. Left: ladder-2's rungs are past `hopUp` (1.5) and the tower's hop to C past `hopSide` (1.5) and to D past `hopDown` (1.5), the owner's sliders; shelves-1's next tier (1 m up, 0.6 m back) has no hang and nothing climbs a waist-high step from its top (phase 6's clamber); a zigzag's A or D alone takes the nearer lip that side, up or down; the crouch held on a ladder of 0.3 m rungs lets go onto the rung under the feet; the shimmy along the tower's D1 does not turn the corner onto D's back face; the round's review found W, S and the crouch tap missing a lip whose face stood 0.25 to 0.3 m (or 0.45 to 0.5 m) behind the held edge, in node as in the tab (the way's columns stood 0.2 m apart in depth, wider than the 0.15 m within which a column takes a face), so the chimney's top was found or not by where the piece stood: the columns are 0.1 m apart in depth now (`WAY_DEPTH_STEP`) and every depth is found with the scenes moved off round places, in node only (the tab was not driven again); a shimmy's look every quarter metre carries on its walk along the held lip (`walkKeep`: about 3.8 rays a frame where it asked 8, 2.9 under 'before'); a grab right after a run that ended let go failed in the tab until the body had stood a few seconds (not looked at); the Controls page's words describe the shipped settings; nothing of it has been seen by eye.

**Animations: Climbing II, Smoothness II** (on `claude/animations`, built on dd88180 by two makers, uncommitted when written; the brief is "Smoothness II brief" in `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`). Built, every change a switch whose other setting is the smoothness round's (`CLIMB_SMOOTH2_BEFORE` under `climb`, `MOVE_SMOOTH2_BEFORE` under `move`; `climbSmooth2Before()` sets them all and `climbSmoothBefore()` calls it, so climbToday's, climbAfter's, climbFeedbackAfter's and climbRows' digests hold): hops, grabs and steps down timed to the clips' own hand moments on a thrown body's arc, drops never played faster than their own rate (`hopFlight`), one-offs crossfaded into the loop after them (`transitions`), the camera eased while climbing (`camera`), each hand on a sloping lip (`slopeHands`) -- maker A's; and the body pushed out of walls by sixteen spheres measured off its own mesh (`bodyClear` 'spheres', `src/player/bodySpheres.ts` and `climbClear.ts`: the trunk, arms and legs push the figure's root out, eased back over `clearEase`, never past `clearMost` nor so far that a placed hand could no longer reach its lip; a reaching hand moved out by its own arm; the duck pushes across only), the duck reading the drawn head over the capsule (`move.duckHead` 'clear', `headSkin`) and lying down under a roof the crouched walk's own head cannot pass, the wall climb's hand over air gripping the nearest real surface (`wallHands` 'grip', `gripSeek`), and Jedi Academy's rebound off a wall in place of the slow jump off (`pushOff` 'jka', `pushBack`, `pushUp`) -- maker B's. On the harness at 30, 60, 144 and 240 (`smooth2Body`, `smooth2Wall`, `smooth2BAlloc`, `smooth2After` whose course digest is cb23818f..., `smooth2Rows` which runs the extra rows with every switch on, digest 50f082fe..., and drives the owner's new pieces, digest c3f1f037...): round every corner, bend and curve the frames with the trunk or a leg over 1 cm in a wall fell from 3,188 to 1,379 at 30 (and alike at the other rates), every placed hand within 1.2 cm of its lip; under beams lowered 5 cm at a time from the head's top to 0.95 m the capsule and the drawn top never past a beam's underside less `headSkin` (in a driven tab the 1.5 m beam's head 5.7 to 7.5 cm clear at the four rates where 'before' walked it 12 cm in, the 1.2 m beam's body lying under it 44 to 63 cm clear where 'before' walked the head 9 cm in); no wall hand grips air for more than 40 ms of one hand in a climb, every placed one within 2 cm of a solid; the push off back at 5.08 m/s and up at 8.53, 5.3 m back off the face 1.5 s on where the jump off made 2.5. Left: round a 60 or 90 degree corner a shin or a thigh still crosses the other face for a moment (the feet's solve plants them on the face held, and the push stops where a hand would leave its lip), and a free hand's clip sweeps through an inner corner's side wall; the brief's "no sphere deeper than 1 cm" is not met there. Its review and fixer round mended, in node and never driven in a browser since: a let-go drop into water (or onto the rope) left the figure frozen in the drop's clip for good, and its held frame stood a frame past the touch by the rate (`driveDropClip` in `hangClips.ts` now holds the player's whole drop hand-over, which the harness's frame calls too, ends the drop the frame the body does anything but fall, holds the rig by a lease it renews every frame, `DROP_LEASE`, and hands it over at `DROP_HOLD_MOST`); after the first deep duck a 1.5 m beam laid the body down for the session, and the first 1.2 m pass of a visit still walked the head 21 cm in: the crouch's own head is read off the crouch clips once a rig, as soon as they are in (`CharacterRig.posedHighest`, `Player.measureDeep`; 1.50 m on the game's human male, whose crouched walk really carries the head that high, 1.35 m on the tests' smaller figure, which the held live read had put 8 cm low), and only a roof low enough to duck the body into the crouch's own clips (`crouchAt`, its band and the room over the capsule) lays it down for that head (`BodyShape.stepUpright`), so in a driven tab the 1.2 m beam laid the body down on its first pass, head 46 to 63 cm clear, and the 1.5 m beam after it was ducked under bent, head 7 cm clear, at 30 and 144; a move's one-off now comes in over `transitionBlend` and a loop still fading in fades out from where it stands (`play`'s `keepWeight`), which took a grab from a run's 2.37 at 144 fps under the measure; and the measure now reaches the climb up, a run grab, a fall caught, a let-go and a wall climb's top-out (`smooth2Trans`). Not built then: the ladder's whole cycle with the hands in turn (5b) and a drop to a lower ledge's own move (6b); Smoothness III built both (below). Of the owner's new pieces the free hops past 1.5 m, the free hops up and the free 1.25 m ladder find no cue at the shipped sliders, the traverses stop at T2, the free climbing walls give E nothing to take, and the braced one is still climbing after 16 s; nothing of it has been seen by eye.

**Animations: Climbing II, Smoothness III** (on `claude/animations`, built on c57c0a5 by two makers and a fixer, uncommitted when written; the brief is "Smoothness III brief" in `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`). Built, every change but item 0 a switch whose other word is c57c0a5's (`CLIMB_SMOOTH3_BEFORE`, `MOVE_SMOOTH3_BEFORE`; `climbSmooth3Before()` sets them and `climbSmooth2Before()` calls it first, so every older digest holds): no drop after topping out (a fault, no switch: the climb up's line-up of the figure and the push its spheres laid on it both go out by its crossfade's own share into the crouch, `Ledge.standKeep`, `LimbPlace.pushKeep`; the lesson above); a hop flies the time a worked-out arc takes, never its clip's window (`climb.hopFlight` 'arc': `flightGravity` 20.32, `arcRise` 0.5, `holdEase` 0.1; the clip fitted to the flight through `moveSeek`, sped up to `flightRateMax` at most, else slowed to `flightRateMin` and held just before its touch), a 3.6 m free hop 0.44 s alike at four rates where its clip's window flew it at 36 m/s; no target whose hanging body would stand in the ground (`climb.targetRoom` 'fit': `hangGround`'s one ray in front of the face, `pathGround` on a hop's way); a drop to the lip beneath on its own move (`climb.dropMove` 'drop': the owner's `DROP_PARTS`, the release and a catch joined on one clock, a 1.5 m drop in exactly the fall's 0.384 s); the duck lies down only into a real opening (`move.proneMin` 'opening': a ball the lying body's thickness and `proneSkin` cast ahead must find the gap running on past the lying body's length, every cast first tested clear of where it begins, measured from the feet: a lying body needs 0.48 m and stalls under 0.479 at 144, so the rule asks 0.49 at the starts and the skin's centimetre over the controller's own offset is the owner's; a lying body's own probe is lifted off the floor by the controller's offset and shrunk a little, or a body lain down under a 0.5 m slab got up and lay down again every frame); the duck wears the highest crouch clip that clears the roof (`move.duckClip` 'clearance', the heads measured on the rig by `measureDeep` by class, `ShapeIn.clipHeads`, handed to the pace and the posture as `duckCls`); a free hang hops up on braced_hang_hop_up's arms over its own legs and drops by maker A's drop move (`climb.freeHops`); and a ladder plays its clip's whole cycle with the hands in turn (`climb.ladderCycle`: the phase carried from rung to rung, the rate within `ladderStretch` of 1, every reaching hand led at its whole weight, `ladderLead` and `ladderArc`, because a hand let go to the clip passed into the rungs and the bodyClear push snapped it out, and a rung's start fading from the wait's own drawn line-up, `cycOf*`, because the hang's step puts its own line-up back earlier in that frame). Its review round mended the push's drop at the hand-back (the hips fell 14 to 17 cm in that frame in a driven tab, and on the shelves rose 12 cm for that frame once the drop was gone; `smooth3Rig` now holds that frame to the frames either side; in a driven tab after it the hips stood 0.859 to 0.867 m over the feet at the hand-back on the braced and free lips and the shelves at 30, 60 and 144, with no step) and the hang's clip hand-over naming a clip and handing a number across a call every frame (32 to 48 bytes; `LEDGE_CLIP_NAMES`, the rig's `timeAt`, `upperAt`, `overrideSeekAt`, `overrideRateAt`, held by `smooth3BAlloc`'s 'hang' part); it found the opening's 0.48 to 0.49 m band the skin's and not a fault (asked from the floor alone, 0.45, the duck laid the body down in front of 0.46 to 0.479 m gaps it could not go into). Measured in node (`smooth3Flight`, `smooth3Rig`, `smooth3Alloc`, `smooth3Ladder`, `smooth3Body`, `smooth3BAlloc`) and in headless tabs in numbers; left of item 5: free hops past 1.5 m (the slider's), the zigzag ladders (a shimmy along a rung stalls), the free climbing wall gives E nothing, the braced one ends in the air, after the first hop on the free 1.25 m ladder the hang reads as braced, and the corner depth, the D hop, the shimmy-start jump, the 30 fps hop camera and the slope-25 hands were not measured; a session's first top-out, before the pack's crouched loop has come in, crossfades into Jedi Academy's own crouch idle (hips 0.42 m over the feet) and rises into the stand 45 cm over three frames at 60 after the hand-back (seen in a driven tab, not mended). How any of it looks is the owner's.

**Animations: Climbing II, phase 3: bars** (on `claude/animations`, built on 955fb04 by two makers, uncommitted when written; the brief is "Phase 3 brief: bars" in `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`). Built, every change a switch whose other word is 955fb04's (`CLIMB_BARS_BEFORE`: `bars` 'off', `barFlight` 'plain'; `climbBarsBefore()`, which `climbSmooth3Before()` calls first, so every older digest's chain holds): the owner's Swinging and Injured Pack folders imported (33 new clips, every clip before byte for byte; `run_and_swing` a one-off with nothing under its feet, the Injured Pack's `injured turn left` and `injured walk backwards` unreadable, "Unknown property type"); a bar is a still collider long, level and thin with room under it for a hanging body where the hands would hold it (`barShape.ts`: `barThin` 0.12, `barLength` 0.8, `roomUnderBar` the one test of the room, the grab's and the ledges'; the ledges' rays pass a bar and only a bar, so a rod along a wall's top or at chest height over the ground is theirs as under 'off', and "No room to hang from the bar here." is said only when no lip or wall takes the press that frame); E grabs it from the ground, a jump or a fall (`BarSwing`, `src/player/barSwing.ts`), a running jump with E playing the clip's reach; the swing a pendulum of the clip's own 1.026 m on the physics' own steps, W and S pumping it at its own phase, A and D along it, its pose the clip's swing window played by the pendulum's angle (`BarSwingTable`, measured off the rig once); Space flies off on the swing's velocity and `barBoost` more (`barFlight` 'fly': the flight held by the bar on the physics' own steps, the standing capsule swept along each step by one shape cast, a bar within `catchReach` ahead of the hands and `catchSlack` of them caught into the reach with the flight's speed carried into the new swing, a bar flown into with the hands over it (at the head's and the arms' height, over the capsule's upper end) caught where it was met, and every other bar but the one let go of as solid to the flight as a wall, else a landing where it meets ground (the clip's own from frame 63, on its twin `BAR_FLIGHT_NAME`), the falling loop at a wall, in water, or falling past `grabFall`, handed to the movement with the fall's memory at the flight's top); the crouch drops straight down; a blow of `barKnock` (25) knocks the body off; other players are told a free hang where its hands are on the grip (`peerPlace`, the rig's state 'hangFree' under the bar's clip); the action bar's "grab", "let go" and "drop". Measured in node (`climbBars`, `climbBarsFly`, `climbBarsAlloc`): on bars-2 to bars-5 a running jump onto the first bar, the swing pumped and Space on its forward swing catch the second and third bars and land on the far platform at every rate, at the start's boost for 2 and 3 m and with it raised to 3 and 4.5 for 4 and 5 m; from one state with the keys on the same steps every rate's flight and catch are the same to the bit; at the start's boost a let-go short of 4 m falls into the pit and lands; the flight's frame makes nothing beyond the engine's two asks a step, nor does a shimmy along the bar (its room asked again each 0.25 m). Left, measured: from one state with the keys on the same steps the flight and the catch are the same at every rate to the bit, and from that state with Space keyed off the swing's angle on each rate's own frames (a press seen up to a 30 fps frame late) the catches at 30 and 240 land 5 to 13 cm apart; end to end from a running jump the review's scripted player lands on the far platform 17 cm to 1.5 m apart at 30, 144 and 240, because the movement's own run-up differs by rate (17 cm along 0.2 s after the jump, so the grab comes at -29 degrees at 30, -36 at 144 and -39.5 at 240) and a press keyed off the swing's angle then lets go a swing sooner at one rate than another (the bar cannot take that out; starting the pendulum at the physics step the bar entered the hands' band would take out only the grab's own frame of lateness, and is not built); the swing's pose crossfades into the flight's release, a body near level, wherever Space was pressed; the wire carries no swing, so others see a still free hang; NPCs do not use bars. How any of it looks and feels is the owner's.

**Animations: Climbing II, phase 4: ropes** (on `claude/animations`, built on 472c90e by two makers, uncommitted when written; the brief is "Phase 4 brief: ropes" in `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`). Built, every change a switch whose other word is 472c90e's (`CLIMB_ROPES_BEFORE`: `ropes` 'off', `ropeLand` 'plain'; `climbRopesBefore()`, which `climbBarsBefore()` calls first, so every older digest's chain holds): the rope's seven clips' rows in the import's table (the three it plays with nothing under their feet, the one-offs trimmed at the owner's frames; the import changed five rows' `trimStart` and no clip file); a rope hangs from an anchor and is no collider (`HangingRope`, `src/player/hangingRope.ts`: its own free swing, a physical pendulum on the physics' own steps, laid as the hook's ribbon; the course's rope piece's column collider switched off while ropes are on and drawn apart, `standCourseRopes`; `__debug.climb({ rope: true })` stands one at the aim); E grabs it standing by it, in a jump or falling past it at the height met, through the game's one use-key dispatch (`climbReach` asks `ropeReach`), refused in words where the hanging body has no room (`RopeSwing`, `src/player/ropeSwing.ts`); on it one pendulum from the anchor on the body's angular momentum, the radius the hands' place, the rope's give (a spring round the stretch the body's weight makes) and the clip's own hang, W and S climbing (stopping at the top, the end and a hand's breadth over the ground, the angular momentum kept), A and D pumping at the swing's own phase to `ropeMost` (`ropeKeys` 'ws' the other keys), the swing's plane turned toward the view, its room asked as one box at the grab and again only as the radius or the plane moves on (a capsule a step while something is near, stopped there); the pose the clips' own (`ropeClips.ts`, the tables measured off the rig once: the hang and the climb on their own clocks, the swing's run played by the pendulum's angle on `start_swinging` from 1.07 s, alternating with a twin), the figure placed with its hands on the rope and its hips on the rope's line (`placeFigure`, `applyRopeRoot`), the clips handed to the rig by `driveRopeClips`; Space lets go on the swing's velocity and the crouch straight down (plain: the movement takes the body). Measured in node (`climbRopes`, `climbRopesAlloc`, `climbRopesAfter`): the swing is alike at 30, 60, 144 and 240 frames a second within a hundredth of a degree; the frames on the rope make nothing. Its second maker's half (items 4 and 5): **Space lets go into a landing where the flight comes to a lip** (`ropeLand` 'lips'): `RopeSwing.release` foresees the flight from the let-go's velocity on the very steps it is flown on (`predict`, the standing capsule swept a step at a time under the swing's gravity, no lower than a landing could be, never into the water), and `classify` names the band by the lip's height over the feet that meet it (a flat top met from above: level, no further down than `ropeLandBelow`; a face met head on: its top found by a ball dropped just in past it, level under `ropeLandKnee`, knee under `ropeLandBetween`, between under `ropeLandWaist`, waist up to `ropeLandTop`, past it a wall), the stand `ropeLandIn` in past the face where the standing capsule fits; the flight held on the physics' own steps (`ROPE_FLY`), a face's landing begun as the band clip's braced foot reaches it (`ROPE_LAND_FACTS.reach`, measured off the human male, as the second its feet are stood), the body carried up the face and then in over the top no faster than `ropeLandWalk`, handed to the movement stood (`landedNow`, the fall's memory the flight's top: `apexY`); no landing, the plain let-go. Its clips (`ropeClips.ts`'s `ROPE_BAND_*`, `measureRopeLandTables`): the flight plays `swinging` from 1.03 s to its foot's touch (its frame before held while the flight is longer), the bands their clips from the owner's seconds, put at the rope's times through the rig's own fields (`driveRopeClips`), let go into the crouch; the figure's hips drawn at the flying body's middle, then its origin eased from there onto the lower ankle stood on the top, and after it the drawn lower ankle held off the top while the offset eases out over the rig's crossfade (`plantY`, `plantFeet`, the player's `plantRopeFeet`), since that crossfade put the feet 0.18 m into the top. Item 5: the camera eased and first person from the head as on any climb (`climbHolds`); a hook rope out refuses the grab and a rope held refuses the hook (maker A's `li.busy` and `climbHolds`); a blow of `barKnock` knocks the body off (`knock`, by the health lost since the last frame on it, as off a bar); the water's swim line lets go into the swim and ends a flight (`swimLine`); other players are sent a free hang's place on the grip (`peerPlace`) and the rope as a held rope whose anchor comes twice, which their game reads as one anchor and draws with no dart and no bite or let-go heard (`PeerRope.hung`); the action bar's words are the bar's (`promptWord`, `reachRope`). The course's rope landings (`climbCourse({ ropeLands: true })`, five pieces, opt-in so no older digest moves; `climbTest()` stands them while `climb.ropes` is 'on' unless `ropeLands` says otherwise, laid for the figure standing). Measured in node (`climbRopesLand`, the four bands and a fall with no lip at 30, 60, 144 and 240 frames a second, the same band and stand at every rate, its own digest; `climbRopesAfter` holds maker A's digest with `ropeLand` 'plain'; `climbRopesAlloc`'s 'land' part holds the flight's and the landing's frames to nothing). Two things cost time. **A big method whose frames reach it only through a short early branch is never taken up again by the optimising compiler once a new path threw its code away**: `RopeSwing.step` and `placeFigure` ran as they stand through every flight after the let-go's own path deoptimised them, so every number their flight branch read made one (16 bytes a frame each); the branch now reads nothing and hands its objects on. **A test that snapshots an object with `Object.assign` takes its lazily made shapes back to null** at every put-back, so the let-go's probe and capsules were made again each time and the stand-in world could not tell its probe; they are copied into the snapshot once made. Its review round mended five things, each a check of `climbRopesLand` that a mutation of it fails: the ropes nobody holds are swung once a frame at the top of the update ahead of every way out (`RopeSwing.tickRopes`, the player's and the harness's), where a rope let go of into a landing hung still at its let-go angle through the whole flight and landing, every rope stood still while the body was on a bar or a ledge, and a grab's frame swung the others twice; the landing's carry is asked for room along its own way, up the face and in over the top, from where the landing will begin (`carryClear`, from `beginOf`'s replay of the flight's very steps) and again where the flight meets the face, where a lintel over the lip had the body carried 0.46 m into it; a stand must have a top under it (`overTop`), where a lintel or a wall too thin to stand on had the body stood in the air beyond it; a top met from above at its very edge, the body's middle out past its face, is landed on in past the edge as a face's is, where it stood on the corner outside; and the course's platforms stand each band's middle over the feet at a 28-degree let-go for the figure standing (`ropeLandTops`, the player's `ledgeScale`), where they sat in the bands' lower parts and the review's figure (the human male at the height slider's top, `figureScaleOf` 1.11) landed each a band lower. Three lessons of it. **Anything a world thing does by itself must be stepped from a place every frame reaches, never from inside what one way of holding the body steps**: the free ropes were stepped from the hang's own frame and the movement's clocks, and every way out of the update before those clocks skipped them. **A landing is asked for three things, never only the stand's room**: the way there, a top under the stand, and that the contact is the kind its branch takes (a top's edge leaning 0.2 to 0.3 fell between the flat top's branch and the face's and was let go the plain way). **The band a lip names hangs on the figure's size and on how long the swing was pumped**: 9 s of A leaves the human male's swing at 33.5 degrees and one a twentieth taller at 35.6, and the flight's last few centimetres fall in the one and rise in the other, so a course can only be laid for a stated let-go, which its labels say. How any of it looks and feels is the owner's: how high each band's lip should be (the edges are sliders), whether the waist clip's feet to the wall read right, and how the drawn hand-over into the crouch looks.

**Animations: Climbing II, the feedback round II, the bar** (on `claude/animations`, built on 5e8f23b by two makers and a fixer, uncommitted when written; maker A's items 0a to 0c of the brief "Feedback round II" in `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`). Built, every change a switch whose other word is 5e8f23b's (`CLIMB_FB2_BEFORE`, `climbFb2Before()`, which `climbRopesBefore()` calls first), every number a slider under the debug menu's `climb`, "Climbing II: Feedback II": **the bar held never stops the camera** (`climb.barCamera` 'pass'): `Physics.cameraBlock` takes a collider to leave out, handed to Rapier's `castRay` as its excluded collider and never a predicate, and the camera's call and the thrown saber's block pass the bar the hands hold (which is also the bar a catch reaches for, taken in the step it is found), and the bar let go of for the length of that flight (`BarSwing.cameraCollider`, `Player.barCamera`), while a wall beside the body still stops every ray; **a swing let alone settles** (`climb.barSettle` 'swings'): with no pump key held its damping is worked out on the physics' own steps from the swing's phase and the pendulum's period at the body's radius, lengthened for the swing's height, so it falls under `barSettleTo` (3 degrees) in `barSettleSwings` (2.5) swings out and back, exactly at 30, 60, 144 and 240 fps in node, the damping only rising during a settle and never above critical, and with W or S held `swingDamp` as before; **a still bar hang is the free hang** (`climb.barHang` 'free'): under `barHangFrom` (4 degrees) with no pump key held the swing's clip crossfades over `barHangBlend` (0.3 s) into the ledges' free hang loop, the hands held on the bar by the limbs' solve (within 1.2 mm through the loop, the crossfades and the shimmy), A and D playing the free hang's shimmy at the shift's own speed, chosen from the keys (judged from the frame's own movement it flickered at 144 fps), and a pump key or the swing past `barHangFrom` crossfades back to the swing played from the pendulum's angle. Other players see the free hang's own rig state and its shimmy; the swing itself is still not on the wire (it would take the bar and the swing's angle in the state). With every switch at its other setting every older digest holds to the bit and the round's bar session is 5e8f23b's (`climbFb2After`, `38b20b43…` over 9,569 frames, recorded from that commit's own code); the round's own checks are `climbFb2Bars` (27) and `climbBarsAlloc`'s settle part. `__debug.climb().bar` reports `camera`, `cameraPasses`, `settle`, `settling`, `hang`, `free`, `freeShare`, `freeLoop` and `freeRate`. The digest's bar session at the shipped settings pumps from the free hang for 4 s and never reaches the let-go's window, so it covers the grab, the settle, the free hang, its shimmy and the pump but not the flight (which `climbFb2Bars` and `climbBarsFly` cover). Seen only in headless tabs of ours: how fast a swing should settle, where the free hang should take over and how long its crossfade, and whether the camera passing through the bar reads right are the owner's.

**Animations: Climbing II, the feedback round II, the free hang and the ladders** (on `claude/animations`, built on 5e8f23b by two makers and a fixer, uncommitted when written; maker B's items 0d to 0g of the brief "Feedback round II" in `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`, the bars' 0a to 0c in the paragraph before). Built, every change a switch whose other word is 5e8f23b's (`CLIMB_FB2_BEFORE`, `climbFb2Before()`), every number a slider under the debug menu's `climb`, "Climbing II: Feedback II, the free hang and the ladders": **a free hang's up cue is a small pull-up** (`climb.freeCue` 'pull'): the hands kept on the grip by the hands' solve while the figure is stood up `freeCueRise` (0.08 m) over `freeCueIn` (0.25 s), its torso the free climb up's first pull laid over the pose (`FREE_CUE_PARTS`, `LimbPlace`'s torso layer, never the rig's upper layer, whose fixed fade let the arms off the grip by 6.8 cm), back down as W is let go, and a hop up begun from it carrying the rise and easing it out by the hands' leave so nothing drops at the commit; **a free hop up kicks its legs** (`climb.freeHopLegs` 'kick'): the free climb up's own leg kick laid under the braced hop's arms, timed to the pull and let go of by the catch, eased in so the commit never snaps the legs; **on a ladder the hips and legs lag the body** (`climb.ladderSway` 'sway'): a damped pendulum stepped on the physics' own coming steps (`Physics.stepsAhead`, `behind`, handed to the ledge by the player and by the harness) and drawn led to the frame's own time, so its lag (8.5 to 8.8 cm on a braced ladder, 16.4 to 16.8 on a free one) and its settle (1.03 to 1.10 s after the key) are alike at 30, 60, 144 and 240 fps, laid on the pelvis about the waist with the spine kept in the world, a braced ladder's feet held where the pose had them (`preFeet`, carried by the root's own push) and a free ladder's legs taking the free hang shimmy's steps, one a reach of the climb cycle; **a free hang looks back and jumps behind** (`climb.freeBehind` 'reach'): the braced look-away's threshold and twist, its pose the free hang's own side hop held at `freeBehindParts.pose` (`FREE_BEHIND_PARTS`), and a jump behind that never takes the lip's own far face (`FREE_BEHIND_PAST`). With every switch at its other setting every older digest holds to the bit and the round's ledge scenes are 5e8f23b's (`climbFb2After`, recorded from that commit's own code before a line was written); the round's own checks are `climbFb2Ledge` (100 checks at four rates on real Rapier with the game's own rig) and `climbFb2LedgeAlloc` (the hang's frame with the cue, the legs and the sway makes nothing, and since the review a free hop up begun from the cue, its carried torso and its leg kick asked of one clip in one frame, makes nothing either: the rig's track cache had been kept by the clip alone and rebuilt both lists every frame of the carry, about 10 KB a frame; twelve processes at once each). `__debug.climb()` reports `freeCue`, `legs`, `sway` and `freeBehinds`. Other players see the rig state the wire already carries (a free hang through the cue, the hop and the look back, and a ladder's climb clips); the cue's rise and torso, the kick, the hips' lag, a free ladder's shimmy legs and the free look-back's held pose are drawn on your screen alone, and showing them would take the cue's share, the legs' clip and weight, the lag and the look's side and share in the state. How any of it looks and feels is the owner's: how far the pull-up rises and how fast, whether the kick reads, how much the hips should swing and how quickly they settle (`swayAmount`, `swayHz`, `swayDamp`), and the free look-away's pose.

**Animations: Climbing II, phase 5: poles** (on `claude/animations`, built on 5421344 by two makers, uncommitted when written; the brief is "Phase 5 brief: poles" in `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`). Built, every change a switch whose other word is 5421344's (`CLIMB_POLES_BEFORE`: `poles` 'off', and maker A's let-go and stop under maker B's three, `poleJump` 'plain', `poleCrouch` 'drop', `poleTop` 'stop'; set by `climbPolesBefore()`, which `climbFb2Before()` calls first, so every older digest's chain holds), every number a slider under `climb`, "Climbing II: poles". **A pole** (maker A, `src/player/barShape.ts`'s `poleAxisOf`, `src/player/poleClimb.ts`) is a still collider no thicker than `poleThin` (0.15 m) over at least `poleLength` (1.5 m), within 10 degrees of upright; the wall climb never takes one under 'on'. **E** in reach grabs it facing it; **W** and **S** climb it on `climbing_a_rope_1` (its row added to `mixamoClips.mjs`; the clip was already in the pack, so nothing was imported), its phase by the way climbed, forward up and backward down, on the physics' own steps; **A** and **D** carry the body round it; the hands are on the axis and each foot pinches it from its own side by the limbs' solve (`LimbPlace.placeFeetAt`, maker A's); the pole held is left out of the camera's block (feedback round II's `barCamera`). **Off the pole** (maker B): **Space** jumps off the way the view looks (`polePush` 3 m/s, `poleJumpUp` 0.5 m), its share toward the pole turned away from it (mirrored across the body's side, the fixer's mend: the camera behind the body looks at the pole, and the flight's sweep leaves that pole out, so a plain Space flew the body through it), flown on the physics' own steps (`POLE_FLY`, the bars' own flight: the capsule swept along each step's parabola, the pole jumped off left out), and on each step another pole in reach is caught as a grab takes one, else a bar or a lip in reach is handed to the bars' or the ledges' own grab through a catcher the player hands the pole (`PoleCatcher`, the use key handed to them as a hop's catch is), a bar given the steps of the frame the flight left (`barStepsOnce`); the crouch slides down at `poleSlide` (3 m/s) to the ground or off the pole's foot, the clip held; **W at the top** climbs onto a lip or a floor's edge in reach (behind the body first) by the ledges' own climb up (`LedgeHang.climbFrom`, its room tests leaving the pole out, `clearSkip`); a blow of `barKnock` knocks the body off, the water lets go into the swim, other players see a free hang's hands on the pole (`PoleClimb.peerPlace`), and the action bar says **E** grab, **Space** jump off and the crouch slide down. The course gains a row after every other, the poles' tops (`poleTops`, stood while poles are on): `pole-top-lip` and `pole-top-floor`. Proved in node through the game's own movement and climbing on the real Rapier at 30, 60, 144 and 240 frames a second (`climbPoles`, `climbPolesOff`, `climbPolesAlloc`, `climbPolesAfter`): with every switch of the round at its other setting climbToday, climbAfter and every older round's digest hold to the bit, and the round's own digest's four scenes hold their 5421344 digests (maker B's three recorded in a scratch copy of that commit's code); the jump from one of the course's poles to the other 2 m off is caught at every rate within 0.7 cm of 60's; nothing is made in a frame, a dozen processes at once. How any of it feels is the owner's: how fast the climb, the turn and the slide go, how far and high the jump off carries, whether a look at the pole should push off backward (the mirror, ours) or sideways along the pole's side (the projection, the other reading), whether the catch should be easier, and whether the 180-degree turn as a climb onto a lip behind the body begins reads well (the ledges' climb faces the lip at once).

**Animations: Climbing II, the rope chain round** (on `claude/animations`, built on 6e59ce8 by two makers, uncommitted when written; the brief is "Rope chain brief" in `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`, the owner's ask of 2026-11-18: "the rope in our climbing pass right now is quite lackluster and is just a straight pipe on a pendulum with no physics or bend"). Built, one switch whose other word is 6e59ce8's (`CLIMB_ROPELOOK_BEFORE`: `ropeLook` 'straight'; `climbRopeLookBefore()`, which `climbPolesBefore()` calls first, so every older digest's chain holds), every number a slider under the debug menu's `climb`, "Climbing II: the rope's chain". Drawing only: every older digest of the body holds to the bit under either word. Maker A's items 1 and 2: **each rope's drawn shape is a chain** (`src/player/ropeChain.ts`, pure: `RopeChain` a rope's, `RopeChains` the frame's driver, kept on the player and the harness) of `ropePoints` points (24) stepped on the physics' own steps (`Physics.stepsAhead`, written at the top of the update beside `tickRopes`) in `ropeSubsteps` substeps (8) by position-based dynamics (Verlet, two sweeps of the links each way, a gentle bend `ropeBend` 0.1, the pins), pinned at its anchor, under the swing's own gravity, damped (`ropeChainDamp` 0.3) and dragged by the trees' and the rain's wind (`FLORA_WIND`, `ropeWind` 0.3), held over the terrain's cached heights (never a read that makes a block) and out of what stands still (one box round the chain a frame, then a ray a point a step where something is near), a tail on the ground lying and sliding to a stop (`ropeGrip` 10); with a body on it the grip is a pin moving along the chain, each of the chain's steps pinned on that very step of the swing's (the swing records itself before the frame's steps and after each, `HangingRope.swingRec`, which nothing of the gameplay reads), the pin begun at a grab and let go at a let-go only between steps, and the rig's two hands (wrist and index knuckle each) are put into the drawn line, the span above eased onto the upper hand and the tail carried with the lower, so the drawn line passes through both hands; let go, the pin goes and the points keep its speed (the whip). Maker B's items 3 and 4: **it is drawn as a strip turned to the view through a smooth curve of the chain's points** (`src/player/ropeCurve.ts`, a centripetal Catmull-Rom, four rings a span, every knot a ring to the bit so the strip meets the hands exactly, a knot lying on something lifted `ropeLift` 0.01 m along its surface's normal; `src/player/ropeChainDraw.ts`, the hook's own camera-facing strip again with its normals bent round the cord, `ropeWidth` 0.04 m, never under the hook's `minPx` pixels across, a twisted rope's strands of our own as a 64 by 16 picture made in code, `ropeLay` 0.14 m a turn, on one material for every chained rope, `CHAIN_ROPE_MATERIAL`, in the hook's rope's colour followed each frame, with a depth offset toward the view whose units are turned under reversed depth), laid for each rope by `drawChained`, the very function the harness lays its strips through; a hidden strip of the material is stood in the scene at the player's making (`chainRopeCarrier`), so the warm-up builds its program behind the first loading screen and no rope stood later builds one; each rope keeps its phase-4 ribbon (made as 6e59ce8 made it) beside its strip, and only one shows. **Another player hanging on a rope here bends it to their drawn hands**: their state already marks a hanging rope by its anchor sent twice (phase 4's `PeerRope.hung`), so the frame loop has the watcher of other players' ropes write each such peer's anchor and their two hands read off their posed rig into the chains' table (`RemoteRopes.hungInto`, `PeerHangs`) before the player's ropes are laid; a rope of this browser's whose anchor stands within `PEER_ANCHOR_NEAR` (0.05 m, five times the wire's centimetre) of theirs is pinned at their hands' middle (`PIN_PEER`, who pins it a change that is a fresh grab), its drawn line through their two hands, and their own straight line to the anchor is then not drawn; the body holding the rope wins over a peer, a peer whose anchor is no rope here is drawn as before, and a frame the game hands no peers (as `__debug.advance`'s) leaves the rope free. Nothing is sent. The console says all of it (`__debug.climb()`'s `ropeChain` and `ropeDrawn`, `handOffMm` how far the strip's rings at the hands stand from the hands as drawn). Measured in node (`climbRopeChain`, `climbRopeChainAfter`, `climbRopeChainDraw`, `climbRopeChainPeers`, `climbRopeChainAlloc`, `climbRopeChainWiring`), through the game's own movement and climbing on real Rapier at 30, 60, 144 and 240 frames a second: with `ropeLook` 'straight' every frame of the body and the rope as drawn is 6e59ce8's to the bit (three scenes, the digests recorded from that commit's own code), and under 'chain' the body is the same to the bit; the chain's points never under the ground or inside the course's pieces by more than a millimetre; the drawn strip's rings at the hands within a centimetre of each hand at every frame of every rate (worst 0.000 mm with the harness's grip, 0.004 mm against the rig's hands in a headless tab at 30, 60 and 240); the free rope's strip the same at 30 and 240 at every whole second of physics steps; a free chain settles to a vertical line; nothing allocated in a frame, a peer's hands and the strip included (twelve processes each). Its review round (the fixer) mended two faults, both measured by the reviewer and not seen by eye. A held chain was not alike by frame rate: each step was pinned on a curve through the frame's two ends and a grab noticed between steps began the pin there, so one swing's chains stepped at 30 and at 240 frames a second parted by up to 1.3 m; now the held session at 30, 144 and 240 replayed step by step into chains taking its steps one, two and four a frame is its own chain to the bit, and its pin stands at the swing's grip after every frame's last step to the bit (`climbRopeChainAlike`). The sessions themselves still differ by rate, by the gameplay's own run (its run-up is stepped by the frame, so the grab, and the whole swing after it, falls elsewhere: the grip up to 2.3 m apart at 30 against 240 and 32 mm at 144), which is phase 4's and was not touched. And with play stood still (a panel, the map, the menu, death: the frame loop steps the physics and draws the ropes but runs no update) the chain was laid at a remainder that moved every frame and jittered up to 30 mm a frame at 144; it is now laid at the remainder of the last frame whose update ran (`RopeChains.drawnBehind`), still to the bit, a free rope and a held one alike. The strip is never inside a piece where the chain's own links round it are clear, but where they cut a corner the rope sweeps across (the chain keeps its points out of what stands still and not its links: up to 77 mm on the course's lying rope sweeping past the platform's edge, in 23 and 49 ring-frames of the two scenes), the curve, laid through the same knots, cuts it too, by at most 8.9 mm past the links; links held out of edges, or more points, would be the chain's own mend. Seen only in a headless tab of our own, in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\wcr-a-*.png`, `wcr-b-*.png`), never by the owner: how the chain hangs, swings and whips (its tail trails and loops at the swing's turn, lightly damped), whether the strands read (the hook's rope colour is dark, 0x3a3229), how thick it should be drawn, and whether a lying rope reads on the ground are his, every one a slider. Not built: the grappling hook's own slack drawn as a chain (a two-ended chain pinned at the hook's pivot and the hand, its length the free span and slack, stepped beside the hook's own frame under its allocation measure, and the peers' hooks drawn the same way), another player's rope that this browser has no rope for (still the straight line to their hand), and the chain's links held out of the edges they sweep across.

**Animations: Climbing II, phase 6: vaults and clambers** (on `claude/animations`, built on 5333b88 by two makers, uncommitted when written; the brief is "Phase 6 brief: vaults and clambers" in `A:\swg3js\anim\DESIGN-CLIMB2.md`, the owner's "great for when sprinting to automatically clamber over obstacles that are appropriate"). Built, one switch whose other word is 5333b88's (`move.vault`: 'sprint' the default, 'off' today, 'run' any run; `CLIMB_VAULTS_BEFORE`, `climbVaultsBefore()`, which `climbRopeLookBefore()` calls first so every older round's digest holds), every number a slider under the debug menu's `move` ("Vaults and clambers (Climbing II, phase 6)"): sprinting into something still whose top stands between `vaultLow` and `vaultHigh` over the feet, with room over it and ground within `vaultFar` beyond, vaults it with the clip whose own hand height is nearest the top (`jumping`, `jumping_1`; `jumping_2` is the same file), its flight stretched to the depth, or past `vaultDeep` dives over it (`jump_over`), and runs on at the speed it came with; a waist-high ledge (`clamberLow` to `clamberHigh`) deeper than `vaultFar` is clambered onto with `running_jump`, cut at its second foot on the top while the sprint is held and played whole otherwise (`src/player/vault.ts`, the clips' measured moments in `src/anim/vaultClips.ts`, the clip on the rig in `hangClips.ts`'s `driveVaultClips`). Never with the placing hand full, swimming, crouched, prone, kneeling, on a vehicle, at a ship's controls or with a hook rope out, nor into something that moves (`VAULT_MOVER_FLAGS`: a vault the still world takes asked again of what moves, refused as `moves`), nor with anything still in the capsule's own corridor (the review's round, `Vault.corridor`: the run-in to the face at the capsule's breadth, and past the far side down to where the feet come down; a post beside the run, a second wall, a bench, refused as `way`); a face too high is the wall as today. The capsule never enters the obstacle (the review's round: raised onto its top, crouched, from the take-off until it is over the face and let down past the far side, a clamber's held at the face while it rises; `rideY`, `holdBack`, `crouchedNow`), the figure drawn where it always was and the chase and the wire following the figure's own way (`Vault.unride`), and the obstacle passes the camera's block while the vault holds the body and its offset eases out (`Vault.cameraCollider` under `climb.barCamera` 'pass': a landing on lower ground just past the top grazed the chase's line for a frame and popped it in six metres and out). While a vault holds the body its hands are full (`climbHolds`: the fight's, use and emote keys muted, the hook refused), the chase follows the figure as a run's does and never the climbing's eased follow, the first-person eye is the drawn head, and the wire carries the nearest pose (`Vault.wireState`: the falling loop stood under the figure's hips in a flight, crouched in a dive's roll and a clamber's climb, standing at a whole clamber's end) with the speed it runs at; no relay restart is needed. Proven in node on real Rapier at 30, 60, 144 and 240 fps (`climbVaults`, `climbVaultsNever`, `climbVaultsAfter`: with `move.vault` 'off' the round's scenes are 5333b88's to the bit, and at 'sprint' the walks and runs into the same pieces are too; `climbVaultsAlloc` twelve processes at once) and in a headless tab of ours in numbers and screenshots (`A:\swg3js\anim\shots\wc6-*.png`); nothing of it has been seen by the owner. How a vault looks and feels, the clip chosen by a top's height, the dive's depth, the clamber's cut, how high a figure bigger than the human male is drawn at the cut, and whether `vaultRateMost` should let `jumping` run at the sprint's own pace are his.

Known gaps and the agreed order for the next work:

1. Wings stand where the game's client data places them and carry their parts, nested foils included; the axis each PSOR angle turns about and its sign (the V-wing's yaw, folding forward; the B-wing's roll, the side its closed wing lies) are the owner's to confirm, and the speed factor's reading (a top-speed cost while open) is ours. So is the wings key's rule that a wing swinging below the belly still waits for room and folds on landing. NPC fighters fly shut and open to fight, and a dock holds the player's ship's wings shut from its approach to the end of its launch, both after the game; the three seconds a fighter keeps them open after a fight are ours, NPC ships do not dock yet (when they do, `holdsWings` is where they join), nobody has yet heard a dogfight of six fighters each sounding its wings as it takes and loses a target, and none of it has been seen by eye. Ships come down and rest on a planet's ground and inside a hangar room, but a ship with rooms of its own still spawns outside a building (boarding one inside a building is its own piece of work), no hull has gear to lower, and a ship setting down out in space flies its settle in the world rather than in the frame of what it is coming down on, so something moving quickly would be landed beside rather than on. A wing set sounds once as it starts to open or close, and every machine runs on the engine loop the game's own files give it, but the boosters' own rocket loop is read and never played, a damaged ship still sounds undamaged although its damage loops are read, and two hulls and six of the gallery's vehicles have no engine sound in the archives at all.
